# Experiments: Feature 80 (desktop server port)

## Baselines

Task 1. Date 2026-10-10. Main checkout, branch `feat/desktop-server-port`, `git rev-parse --short HEAD` = `5c9b49f0`, tree clean at the start (`git status --short` printed nothing). `node --version` = v24.15.0 for every command (the CI Node 22 and Node 20 were not used; no Node 20 or CI result was seen). Unedited tree: no source change. Recorded by the implementor of Task 1.

### Pre-checks and process safety

Before any server: `lsof -iTCP:3000`, `6006` and `7340 -sTCP:LISTEN` printed nothing (all free). Re-checked 3000 and 7340 before the build and 3000 before the smoke run: free. The owner's `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` was not read, listed or written.

### Sandbox use

Inside the command sandbox: `pnpm test:ci`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` (frontend/), `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck` (electron/), `pnpm knip` (repo root, `COLUMNS=400`), `pnpm --filter getwrite-cli test`, git reads. Outside the sandbox (`dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true`): `pnpm build`, the `.next/static` copy, `pnpm test:sharing-smoke`, `pnpm test:e2e`, and the port checks around them. The only stderr noise was the pnpm `~/.npmrc` EPERM warning. One CLI-run message: the sandbox printed `sysmon request failed with error: sysmond service not found` during the CLI suite.

A first attempt to run the build and smoke used a log path under the sandbox `$TMPDIR`, which does not exist outside the sandbox; the redirect failed, `pnpm build` was not run by that attempt, and `.next/static` was copied from whatever `.next` already held. The build, copy and smoke were then re-run from scratch with a log path outside `$TMPDIR` (the results above are from that second run only).

### e2e and the Storybook process

6006 had no listener before the run. `pnpm test:e2e` does not need a Storybook started by hand: the Playwright config's `webServer` runs `pnpm run build-storybook && node scripts/serve-storybook-static.mjs` on 6006 (`reuseExistingServer: true`). A background `lsof` poll every 3 s during the run saw exactly one listener pid on 6006: 929. That server was started by Playwright's `webServer`, not by a separate command of mine, so no pid was stopped by me. After the run `lsof -iTCP:6006 -sTCP:LISTEN` printed nothing (the server was gone), and no storybook or serve-storybook process was found with `ps`. The task did not start `pnpm storybook` (dev) at all.

## Inventory of 3000

Task 2. Date 2026-10-10. Main checkout, branch `feat/desktop-server-port`, `git rev-parse --short HEAD` = `aa495f93`, tree clean at the start (`git status --short` printed nothing). `node --version` = v24.15.0 (used only for the one-line `new URL` check below). Recorded by the implementor of Task 2. Everything here ran **inside the command sandbox** (reads, `rg`, `git ls-files`, `ls`, `sed`, `python3 -I` for the classification table, one `node -e`); no process was started, no port was bound, and `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` was not read or listed. Code facts below are **read, not run** unless a line says "measured". No cause is named for anything that was not discriminated by a check.

### Summary (facts from the search below)

- 337 hits of the whole word `3000` in 65 tracked files. Classified one by one in "Classification and disposition of every hit": class (a) 1, (b) 145, (c) 0 literal hits (class (c) items have no literal and are listed separately), (d) 154, (e) 37. Dispositions: changed 1 (`electron/src/server-config.ts:11`, Task 8), changed at the documentation stage 1 (`CLAUDE.md:219`), left 120, left with a comment added by Task 11 1, left as provisional on OQ-2 9, left as fixtures that pass unchanged 89, historical 116.
- Hits that follow the single source without containing the literal (class (a), found by searching for `PORT`): 11 lines in `electron/src/main.ts`, 1 in `electron/src/sharing/addresses.ts`, and 3 server-side readers of the `PORT` env, plus 8 test lines that build an origin from `PORT` (listed in "Class (a) without the literal").
- Class (c) items (a started process defaults to 3000 with no literal, a Hypothesis): `frontend/package.json:6` and `:9`, root `package.json:7` and `:8`, `electron/src/main.ts:222`, `demo/playwright.demo.config.ts:24`, and `scripts/hosted-smoke/README.md:72`.
- No port computed from another value was found by the patterns used (see commands 7 to 9). Two free-port probes (`listen(0, ...)`) exist and are unrelated to the constant.
- Differences from the FR-6 starting list: 11 listed under "Difference from the FR-6 starting list"; one of them is a measured defect-in-waiting for Task 8: with `PORT = 7340`, `localhost:${PORT}1` in `electron/tests/device-ipc.test.ts` and `limits-ipc.test.ts` is `http://localhost:73401/`, which `new URL` rejects (measured, Node v24.15.0), so those two "lookalike port" cases would stop exercising a lookalike origin even though they would still pass.

### Scope and method

- **File set searched.** The 2152 git-tracked files (`git ls-files`, 2154) minus `pnpm-lock.yaml` and `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md` (the file this section is written into; excluding it avoids counting this record, and its Task 1 section mentions ports 3000, 6006 and 7340 only as pre-check targets, not as a setting). Untracked and ignored paths (`node_modules`, `.next`, `dist`, `out`, `.native-build`, `storybook-static`, `*.tsbuildinfo`, `.playwright-mcp/`, `.DS_Store`, `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.husky/_/`) are therefore **not searched**; that is the exclusion list and the reason for it (build output and tool state are not source of truth). `.claude/skills/` and `.github/` are tracked and were searched.
- **A first pass was discarded.** A plain `rg -n -w '3000' .` with the default ignore rules was started first; it does not search hidden directories, so it returned no `.github/workflows/` hits (334 lines in 64 files). The git-tracked-list method replaced it. Its output is not recorded here.
- **The wrapper.** `$S/g.sh` (scratchpad, not committed) is: `cd <repo>; git ls-files -z | grep -zv -e '^pnpm-lock.yaml$' -e '^frontend/pnpm-lock.yaml$' -e '^specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md$' > files.z; xargs -0 rg -n --no-heading --with-filename "$@" < files.z | sort -t: -k1,1 -k2,2n`. Every command below is `g.sh <rg args>`.
- **A flag that did nothing.** Commands 6 and 8 and 10 were run with `--glob '!specs/**'` (and similar). With an explicit file list from `xargs` the glob did not filter; the outputs include `specs/` paths. The outputs are recorded as produced.
- **Readiness file path (confirmed by `ls`).** `ls electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts electron/src/server-readiness.ts` printed `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` and `ls: electron/src/server-readiness.ts: No such file or directory`. The file is `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` (1747 bytes).

Command 5 (`\bPORT\b`) also lists the spec text that mentions the variable; it is pasted in full.

### One measured check

`node -e 'for (const p of [3000,7340]) { try { console.log(p, new URL("http://localhost:"+p+"1/").origin) } catch(e){ console.log(p, "throws", e.code||e.message) } }'` printed (Node v24.15.0, 2026-10-10):

```
3000 http://localhost:30001
7340 throws ERR_INVALID_URL
```

This is a measurement of Node's URL parser only; it did not run the tests.

#### 1c. Docs (11)

~~~~text
CLAUDE.md:219:- On launch, resolves repo root (or `process.resourcesPath` when packaged) and spawns the frontend's Next.js **standalone** server (`frontend/.next/standalone`) as a child process on port 3000 (`electron/src/server-config.ts`'s `PORT`, the single source the bind env, window load URL, readiness check, navigation guard, IPC sender check and displayed addresses all read; Feature 80 will change the value), then opens a `BrowserWindow` pointed at it.
README.md:106:| `BETTER_AUTH_URL`                                               | better-auth's `baseURL` — this deployment's own canonical URL. Falls back to `http://localhost:3000` for local development, but real deployments (hosted and self-host) **must set it explicitly, and to an `https://` URL**: better-auth derives the session cookie's `Secure` flag from this protocol, so an `http://` value ships session cookies without `Secure`. Also the source of the allowed `Origin`/`Referer` for the CSRF check on state-changing tenant routes.  |
demo/README.md:17:- Dev server running on :3000 (`pnpm start` from repo root). The Playwright
demo/README.md:76:- `playwright.demo.config.ts` — drives :3000 (not Storybook), video on, 1440×900.
docs/api/openapi.yaml:9:    Base URL: `http://localhost:3000` (development)
docs/api/openapi.yaml:25:  - url: http://localhost:3000
docs/features/home-network-sharing.md:73:- The window may navigate only to the exact local origin (`isLocalOriginUrl` compares parsed origins; the previous `startsWith` test let `http://localhost:3000@evil.example/` and `http://localhost:30001/` through when evaluated in Node).
docs/features/home-network-sharing.md:173:- IPv6 is not in scope. Under a `0.0.0.0` bind, `curl -6 http://localhost:3000/` did not connect while `curl -4` did (cause not established).
docs/features/indexing.md:142:await flushIndexer(); // optional timeout: await flushIndexer(3000);
scripts/hosted-smoke/README.md:60:BETTER_AUTH_URL='http://localhost:3000' \

specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:28:> development defaults (3000, 4200, 5000, 5173, 6006, 7000, 8000, 8080, 8888,
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:48:The desktop app starts a bundled server on port 3000, a common default that
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:55:holds or assumes 3000, decides each one (several decisions are open below),
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:62:- Every address the app shows and every check the server makes follows the one port source; no consumer of the desktop port keeps 3000 by accident.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:63:- Every place that holds or assumes 3000 is listed from a recorded search and has a recorded disposition: follows the source, changed, deliberately left, or historical.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:72:- TLS, a redirector listening on 3000, or any new network listener.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:88:FR-1: The constant `PORT` in `electron/src/server-config.ts` (read, not run: line 11, `export const PORT = 3000;`) MUST become 7340. No other source file in `electron/src/` MUST gain a numeric port literal. The port is a fixed constant (OQ-7, resolved): no environment override and no writer-facing setting MUST be added. [US-1]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:92:FR-3: Each consumer of the desktop port MUST keep reading it from the single source, and a unit test MUST show it with a port other than 3000 or 7340 passed in as input. The consumers, read, not run: the server's `PORT` env (`electron/src/main.ts` line 173); the window load, retry and readiness URLs (lines 732, 738, 745); the navigation guard (line 683); the IPC sender checks (lines 292, 300, 304); the window-secret installation (line 782); the sharing status port (line 313) and `candidateAddresses(interfaces, port)` in `electron/src/sharing/addresses.ts`. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:96:FR-5: The task list MUST begin with a recorded search for 3000 used as a port, in a new `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md`: the exact command, its full output, and a classification of every hit as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal (for example `next dev` and `next start` with no `PORT`, which default to 3000: Hypothesis from Next's documented behaviour, to be settled by reading the dev scripts and by FR-12's run), (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, a word count, a test count). The search MUST cover source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`, and MUST also look for ports written without the literal. The list in this spec below is from a search made while writing it and is a starting point, not the record. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:98:FR-6: Hard-coded elsewhere, from that search (read, not run), each needing a recorded disposition under FR-24: `frontend/src/lib/auth/auth-server.ts` line 273 and `frontend/better-auth.config.ts` line 86 (`http://localhost:3000` when `BETTER_AUTH_URL` is unset); `frontend/app/api/_tenant/with-storage-context.ts` lines 155 and 165 (the same fallback for the allowed Origin); tests asserting those fallbacks (`frontend/tests/unit/auth-server.test.ts` line 265, `better-auth-cli-config.test.ts` line 61); `README.md` line 106 (the fallback, documented); `scripts/hosted-smoke/smoke.mjs` line 36 and its README; `docs/api/openapi.yaml` lines 9 and 25; `demo/seed.mjs`, `demo/playwright.demo.config.ts` and `demo/README.md` (the dev server on 3000); `CLAUDE.md` line 219 (the Electron section says 3000); `docs/features/home-network-sharing.md` lines 73 and 173 (examples in recorded measurements); `.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml` lines 67, 94 and 138; `cli/src/qa/server.ts` and `cli/tests/qa/server.test.ts` (a comment and a throwaway occupier on 3000; the harness picks a free port itself); the scripts under `specs/features/paired-device-limits/scripts/`. Test files that use 3000 only as a fixture value passed in as input (the gate, same-origin, host-allowlist and pair-route tests, `host-allowlist.cases.json`, `electron/tests/addresses.test.ts`, `window-secret.test.ts`, `navigation-guard.test.ts`) follow FR-25 of Feature 75 and MUST pass unchanged. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:100:FR-7: The linux AppImage smoke workflow MUST NOT be pointed at 7340 on the strength of this feature alone (OQ-9, resolved). Read, not run: it downloads the published releases 1.8.0 and 2.0.0 (`gh release download getwrite-v${{ matrix.version }}`) and probes `http://localhost:3000`; those builds were released with 3000 and the workflow tests them. The only workflow change MUST be a comment near its "nothing may answer on :3000" check (`.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml`, around line 138) saying that a future matrix entry for a build on 7340 needs its own probe port; no probe, matrix or step is changed. The disposition is recorded under FR-24. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:102:FR-8: The scripts under `specs/features/paired-device-limits/scripts/` are historical records. Read, not run: they refuse to run when port 3000 has a listener and `electron-limits.mjs` line 81 sets `BASE` to `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, so against a build with 7340 they would test the wrong address. They MUST NOT be edited by this feature (OQ-8, resolved); the new experiments file MUST say they are not valid against the new port and why. [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:110:FR-12: Development mode MUST follow OQ-2, whose direction is provisional (accepted by the owner for writing the task list and brought back at the FR-23 stop): `pnpm electron:dev` follows the constant (it already inherits `PORT`) and plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; a separate development port (7341) is an optional task the stop can turn on. Read, not run: `pnpm electron:dev` runs `electron dist/main.js`, whose `startServer` spawns `pnpm dev` in `frontend/` with the same env including `PORT` (`main.ts` lines 224 to 228), and `cli/src/qa/server.ts` documents that `next dev` honours `PORT`; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` run `next dev` with no port argument (`package.json`, `frontend/package.json` line 6). The disposition of each of `pnpm dev`, `pnpm start`, `pnpm electron:dev` and the demo scripts MUST be recorded, and a run MUST show which port a started dev server actually listens on. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:112:FR-13: Hosted-only behaviour follows OQ-3 (resolved, confirmed by the owner, Gate 3, 2026-10-10): the hosted-auth fallbacks (`auth-server.ts`, `better-auth.config.ts`, `with-storage-context.ts`) stay at `http://localhost:3000`, and those files and the tests that assert them MUST be left unchanged, and the hosted web app MUST NOT be affected by this feature. (Read, not run, from the project notes: with hosted auth inactive `with-storage-context.ts` skips its session and Origin checks, and sharing and hosted auth never run together, so the desktop server does not reach these fallbacks; this MUST be re-read from the code during the task, not relied on.) [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:118:FR-16: A device that opens the old address MUST be examined, not assumed: with nothing listening on 3000 (checked first), the record MUST state what a real Chromium and, by the owner, the Android phone's browser show. If a development server is also running on 3000 (OQ-2), the record MUST state whether it answers a request from the host's LAN address with project data, because the record of Feature 76 shows a `next dev` server did so with sharing off (measured then, from the host, not a second device; whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked). What is done about it follows OQ-5, whose direction is provisional: a release-note or docs line only, no redirector or listener on 3000, and text in the sharing panel only if this record shows the failure is confusing. [US-3]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:120:FR-17: With sharing on, the Host allowlist MUST accept `<lan-ip>:7340`, `localhost:7340` and `127.0.0.1:7340` and MUST refuse `<lan-ip>:3000` and `localhost:3000`, shown against a built server started on a free port set through `PORT` and, separately, against the real app. A request sent from the host to its own LAN address is not a second-device result and MUST be labelled so. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:122:FR-18: With sharing on, every address shown in the desktop window (the status and settings surfaces) MUST end in `:7340`, and none MUST end in `:3000`, shown in the real Electron app in development mode and in an unpacked build, each with a throwaway `--user-data-dir` and a throwaway projects folder. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:124:FR-19: In the same two real-app runs, with sharing off and with sharing on, the window MUST load its content from `http://localhost:7340`, and a request to `http://localhost:3000` MUST NOT reach the app's server (checked only after confirming nothing else listens on 3000). [US-1]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:130:FR-22: Port in use MUST be measured twice, and each record MUST say which port it used and why. First, before the constant changes, today's mechanism on 3000 with a throwaway listener (valid for the mechanism, because the readiness and fork code is the same for any port; it says nothing about 7340 itself). Then again on the changed build on 7340. For the 7340 run: the task starts its own throwaway listener on 7340 (after confirming nothing else listens there), launches the real app in development mode and in an unpacked build against throwaway folders, and records what the window shows, whether the window loaded the listener's response, whether the listener received the window header (recorded as present or absent only, never its value), the app's exit and log lines, and what the sharing status reports. The listener MUST be stopped by its own recorded process id. Results go to the owner before FR-11's behaviour is built. [US-4]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:136:FR-25: Documentation MUST be updated in the same change, with each statement dated and labelled read, measured or reported: `CLAUDE.md` (the Electron Shell section's port, and the sharing text if it states 3000); `docs/features/home-network-sharing.md` (a dated note giving the current value, leaving the recorded measurements as they were); the README and user-facing docs for any place that tells a writer an address; the `openapi.yaml` development base URL per the FR-12 disposition. The feature list's Feature 80 status is updated by the owner's process, not by the implementer. [US-5]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:140:FR-27: Process safety. Any task that starts a server or app MUST: check the app's port (7340, and 3000 when a development server may be involved) for a listener first and refuse to run if one exists; start servers on free ports it picks itself where the check does not need 7340; stop only the process ids it started and recorded, never by port and never a listener's parent; use throwaway user-data and projects folders; never read or write `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/`; and never print or record credentials, cookies, secrets or pairing codes. (On 2026-10-10 a pipeline script stopped every listener on port 3000, including the owner's running desktop app; PDM experiments, "A run that does not count, and a mistake".) [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:156:- OQ-2 (provisional, open until the FR-23 stop): Does development mode move to 7340, stay on 3000, or take its own port in the block, and should a packaged app and a dev app be able to run at once? Evidence: read, not run, `electron:dev` already passes `PORT` to `next dev`, so it follows the constant; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` do not and default to 3000; the demo scripts and `demo/README.md` assume 3000; the QA harness picks a free port and is unaffected; whether the packaged and dev apps share a single-instance lock was not established. Options: (a) `electron:dev` follows the constant, `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; (b) all of them move to 7340 (`next dev -p`), so only one runs at a time; (c) dev takes a second port in the block (7341) so both run at once, with the demo scripts following; (d) all stay on 3000 and `electron:dev` is given its own override. Trade-off: (a) leaves the 3000 collision the owner wants to avoid in the everyday dev command; (b) removes it and forbids running both; (c) allows both and spends a number in the block. Provisional direction (accepted by the owner for writing the task list, to be brought back at the FR-23 stop): (a), with (c) an optional task the stop can turn on depending on whether a dev app and a packaged app can run at once (single-instance lock keying is unverified). — Impact: FR-12, FR-16, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:158:- OQ-3 (resolved): hosted-auth fallbacks stay at `http://localhost:3000`; those files and their two tests are unchanged; see FR-6, FR-13, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:162:- OQ-5 (provisional, open until the FR-23 stop): What should a device that still opens `:3000` see, and does anything need to say so? Evidence: nothing in the app listens on 3000 after the change (to be shown, FR-16); the real browsers' error page is the likely result (Hypothesis); a development server on 3000 (OQ-2) could answer instead and was observed once to answer from the LAN address with sharing off. Options: (a) nothing, document the new address; (b) a release-note line; (c) the desktop window's sharing panel says the old address no longer works; (d) a tiny listener on 3000 that redirects (adds a network listener on the commonly used port, against the purpose). Provisional direction (accepted for writing the task list, to be brought back at the FR-23 stop): a release-note or docs line only; no redirector or listener on 3000; panel text only if FR-16 shows the failure is confusing. — Impact: FR-16, FR-25.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:170:- OQ-9 (resolved): the AppImage smoke workflow keeps probing 3000, with a comment only; see FR-7, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:176:- OQ-1 (resolved): What should happen when the port is already in use at launch? Resolution: option (a). The app refuses to start the server and shows a plain message naming the port; there is no fallback port. Independently, the readiness check must accept only the server the app itself forked. Evidence (read, not run): `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` lines 49 to 53 resolve on any status below 500 and send the window secret header on every poll; `main.ts` raises "Server exited unexpectedly" only on a non-zero exit. Hypothesis, not yet measured: a foreign listener answering below 500 passes readiness and receives the header (FR-22 measures it, first on 3000 with a throwaway listener to establish the mechanism, then again on 7340 after the constant changes). The window secret must never be sent to a process the app did not start. The mechanism (for example a per-launch nonce the child echoes, or the child's exit or bind state) is a design choice for the task; the size of that work is to be confirmed by the FR-22 measurement. Impact: FR-10, FR-11, FR-22.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:177:- OQ-3 (resolved): Do the hosted-auth fallbacks stay at 3000? Resolution: yes (option a). `auth-server.ts`, `better-auth.config.ts`, `with-storage-context.ts` and their two tests are unchanged. Impact: FR-6, FR-13, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:181:- OQ-9 (resolved): Does the AppImage smoke workflow change? Resolution: it keeps probing 3000 because it tests the published 1.8.0 and 2.0.0 releases; a comment near its "nothing may answer on :3000" check says a future matrix entry for a 7340 build needs its own probe port. No other workflow change. Impact: FR-7, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:13:- **Process-safety rules, repeated by every task that starts a server or app (FR-27).** Before starting anything: check for a listener on the port the app will use (3000 before Task 8 lands, 7340 after) and on every port the task itself uses (`lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN`), and REFUSE to run if the app's port is busy, unless the task itself created that throwaway listener (Tasks 3 and 6 and the port-in-use steps of 17 and 19). Servers the task starts use a free port the task picks itself unless the task is checking 7340 or 3000 on purpose. Record the pids the task starts and stop only those pids, never by port, never a listener's parent or a process the task did not start (on 2026-10-10 a pipeline script stopped every listener on port 3000, including the owner's running desktop app). Use throwaway user-data and projects folders under `$TMPDIR`, assert the paths in the script before using them, and NEVER read or write `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/`. Never print or record credentials, cookies, secrets, window-secret values or pairing codes; header presence is recorded as present or absent only. Scripts live under `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/` (not under `frontend/`, so `pnpm knip` does not see them) and are described in `experiments.md`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:17:- **Port statement.** Measurements before Task 8 can only be made on 3000, the port the code uses until the constant changes; measuring the port-in-use mechanism on 3000 with a throwaway listener is valid for the mechanism and says nothing about 7340. After Task 8 the same measurement is repeated on 7340 (Tasks 17 and 19).
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:18:- Do NOT change: the hosted-auth fallbacks (`frontend/src/lib/auth/auth-server.ts`, `frontend/better-auth.config.ts`, `frontend/app/api/_tenant/with-storage-context.ts`) and their two tests; `device-credentials.json` handling or the credential-store schema; the scripts under `specs/features/paired-device-limits/scripts/`; any workflow other than the comment in Task 11; `frontend/next.config.mjs`. No migration code. No new listener on any port; no redirector on 3000. Tests that use 3000 only as a fixture value passed as input MUST pass unchanged (FR-6).
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:28:**Done when:** the section records, with command, date, exit status, counts, `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `node --version`: from `frontend/`, `pnpm test:ci` (passed, failed, skipped), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` (errors, warnings), `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` (checks and failures; after `pnpm build` and copying `.next/static` into `.next/standalone/frontend/.next/static`), and `pnpm test:e2e` (passed, failed, skipped, against `pnpm storybook` on 6006, with the Storybook pid started by the task and stopped by that pid); from `electron/`, `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; from the repo root, `pnpm knip` with the issue count per section AND the list of names (so later runs are compared by set), and `pnpm --filter getwrite-cli test`. The record also states the reported reference figures (Feature 77: frontend 6434 passed and 1 skipped, desktop 384, CLI 144, smoke 28 checks, e2e 214 passed and 1 skipped) and marks them "reported, not re-run" unless the run reproduces them. A failing baseline suite is recorded as failing with its test names and not fixed. Before starting, the task checks 3000, 6006 and 7340 for listeners and refuses if 6006 is busy; any server it starts uses a free port it picks, and it stops only the pids it started. `git status --short` afterwards shows no change outside `specs/`, apart from the spec work already present at the start (paste the output).

specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:37:**Files:** Appends section "Inventory of 3000" to `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md`. No source change. Reads `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/`, `.github/workflows/`, `scripts/`, `demo/`, `cli/`, `electron/`, `frontend/`, `specs/`, `package.json` files, and `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` (confirm by `ls` that the readiness file's path is `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts`, not `electron/src/server-readiness.ts`).
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:38:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records the exact commands (for example `rg -n -w '3000'` with the excluded directories named, plus searches for `localhost:3000`, `127.0.0.1:3000`, `:3000`, `PORT`, `-p 3000`, `--port`, `process.env.PORT`, `next dev`, `next start`, and ports written without the literal such as `3e3` or a port computed from another value) and their FULL output, covering source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`. Every hit is classified as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal, (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, word count, test count), and ends with a disposition (follows the source, changed, deliberately left, or historical) and a reason. The starting list of FR-6 is checked against the result and every difference is listed, including any hit the list missed. The expected dispositions per the Gate 3 answers are recorded as owner decisions: hosted-auth fallbacks and their two tests left; the `paired-device-limits` scripts left (historical, not valid against the new port, and why); the AppImage smoke workflow left except for a comment (Task 11). Every "changed" disposition names the test or check that will cover it (FR-24). `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:45:### Task 3: Experiment (agent): port in use TODAY, measured on 3000 with a throwaway listener
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:46:**What:** Measure what the unchanged app does when something already listens on its port, on 3000 (the only port the unchanged code uses), so the owner has the mechanism before Tasks 9 and 10 are sized.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:47:**Files:** Appends section "Port in use today (3000)" to `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md`; script `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/port-in-use-today.mjs`. Reads `electron/src/main.ts` (the fork at ~173 and ~209, the "Server exited unexpectedly" path ~815, `requestSingleInstanceLock`, `installWindowSecret` ~782), `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` (lines 49 to 53), `electron/src/sharing/window-secret.ts`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:48:**Done when:** `experiments.md` states in its first lines WHICH PORT this used and why: 3000, because the constant has not changed yet; valid for the mechanism; says nothing about 7340; Task 17 and Task 19 repeat it on 7340. It records, using the dev-mode copied-tree approach and a throwaway `--user-data-dir` and projects folder: that 3000 had no listener before the task's own throwaway listener started (the task refuses to run otherwise); the throwaway listener is a tiny HTTP server on 3000 answering a distinct marker page with status 200, started by the task, its pid recorded, which records only the PRESENCE (yes/no) of the `x-getwrite-window` request header on each request it receives and never the value; for the app launched against it: what the window showed (screenshot description or window title and a DOM text excerpt that contains no secret), whether the window loaded the listener's page (yes/no), whether the listener received the window header (present or absent only, and how many requests), whether the app showed "Server exited unexpectedly" or any other message, the forked server child's exit code and the app log lines (names and messages only), what the sharing status reported, and whether the app exited. A second variant with a listener answering status 500 and a third with no response at all (accepts the connection and stays silent) are recorded the same way if they run without extra setup. The reading in FR-10 ("resolves on any status below 500, sends the secret on every poll") is stated as read, and the run is stated as the measurement; a cause is named only where a variant discriminates it. The record ends with a plain statement for Tasks 9 and 10 of which of these hold today: the window showed a foreign page; the header reached a foreign process; a failed bind of the child is or is not visible to the app. The app and listener are stopped by their recorded pids and the throwaway folders removed. `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:51:**Notes:** Satisfies FR-10 (measurement), FR-22 (first half, mechanism). Main checkout, outside the sandbox (Electron needs it). Process-safety rules apply in full; the only listener on 3000 is the task's own. Never read the real `userData`. The nonce-versus-child-state choice in Task 9 uses this result.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:58:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records, per command, the port it LISTENS on measured by `lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -a -p <pid>` for the pid the task started: `pnpm dev` (frontend), `pnpm start` (alias for the frontend dev), and `pnpm electron:dev` in the copied-tree approach on today's code (3000); plus `next dev` with `PORT` set to a free port the task picks (to show `PORT` is honoured; the QA harness comment is read, this is the run); the demo scripts are read for the port they assume and marked "read, not run". For coexistence it records, by reading and by a run: how `requestSingleInstanceLock` is keyed (Electron keys it by `userData` path; stated as read from the Electron documentation if not run), and the result of starting two instances of the dev-mode copied-tree app with two DIFFERENT throwaway `--user-data-dir`s (the second quits or not; whether the second's server bound or failed), with the facts only. It states what is not established (for example the behaviour of a packaged build and a dev build together, which Task 19 can add to). All servers are started on 3000 only when the task has confirmed 3000 free, otherwise the task refuses; pids recorded and stopped by pid; throwaway folders removed. `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.

specs/features/destructive-styling-a11y/tasks.md:500:   local dev server is already bound to port 3000 — stop that process
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:153:FR-25: The server port MUST have a single source that the server's bind, the desktop window's load URL and navigation guard, and the displayed address all read. No requirement or test in this feature MAY depend on the port having any particular value. (Read: `electron/src/main.ts` today holds `PORT = 3000` and the load URL and `will-navigate` guard use `http://localhost:${PORT}`; choosing a new value is Feature 80.) [US-1]
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:165:FR-31: With sharing on, a request from any client other than the desktop window MUST be refused unless its `Host` header names this machine, compared case-insensitively, as `<name>:<port>` where `<port>` is the server's port (FR-25) and `<name>` is one of: `localhost`; `127.0.0.1`; an IPv4 address currently assigned to one of the machine's own network interfaces; or `<hostname>.local`, where `<hostname>` is the machine's hostname lowercased with a trailing `.local` removed. A missing `Host`, a `Host` without the port, a wrong port, an IPv6 literal, and any other name MUST be refused. The check applies to every request that is not the window's, including the exempt requests (the `/pair` page, `POST /api/sharing/pair`) and `/_next/static` assets: the gap measured by the security review was a request with `Host` and `Origin` both `evil.example:3000` passing `isSameOrigin` in the gate and in the pair route (review L1, measured at gate level; this is the DNS-rebinding shape: a page on a foreign name reaching the pairing endpoint). A refusal MUST be visible, in text, saying that the address does not name the computer running GetWrite and to open the address shown in the GetWrite window (working copy, pending owner confirmation: "This address does not name the computer running GetWrite. Open the address shown in the GetWrite window on that computer."); it MUST NOT redirect to the pairing screen, because that same request would also be refused there. Design (Owner decision, 2026-10-09, "Both confirmed, go ahead"): the allowed set is computed by the server process itself, from `os.networkInterfaces()` and `os.hostname()`, at request time (or cached for a bounded short time), not fixed at launch, so an address change needs no restart. The addresses the desktop window displays (FR-6) and the set this check accepts MUST come from one rule: both start from the machine's non-internal IPv4 interface addresses; the window shows the private-range subset, as FR-6 says, and every address the window shows MUST be accepted by this check. The window shows the IP-address URLs first. Consequences, stated plainly: (a) a paired device's cookie belongs to the host it was set on (general browser behaviour, not tested here), so if the desktop's address changes the device must be opened at the new address and paired again; (b) a `<hostname>.local` name avoids that where the device can resolve it, which is not assured on every Android version (not measured here), and whether the `<hostname>` that `os.hostname()` returns is the name the device resolves is also not measured on any platform; (c) a router address reservation is the dependable remedy for a changing address; (d) a name the writer's router or a reverse proxy supplies (for example `nas.lan`) is refused. IPv6 addresses are not in scope: nothing in this feature listens on IPv6 (Measured in the experiments, `experiments.md` section C run 2, and recorded in Task 24 item 14: `localhost` over IPv6 did not connect under a `0.0.0.0` bind while IPv4 did; cause not established). Provenance: Security review finding, 2026-10-09; owner decision to fix, 2026-10-09. [US-1]
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:173:FR-35: The window MUST be navigable only to the local origin: a navigation MUST be allowed only when the parsed URL's origin equals the local origin exactly. The sharing IPC handlers (`getwrite:sharing-get-status`, `-set-enabled`, `-generate-code`, `-get-code`) MUST refuse a call whose sender frame's origin is not the local origin. Evaluated in Node, not tested in Electron: the current `will-navigate` handler uses `url.startsWith(localOrigin(PORT))`, and `http://localhost:3000@evil.example/` and `http://localhost:30001/` both pass that prefix test while their parsed origins are `http://evil.example` and `http://localhost:30001`; the prefix test predates this branch. The bridge on this branch exposes `generatePairingCode`, `getPairingCode`, `setSharingEnabled` and `restart`, and the IPC handlers do not check the sender (Read, `electron/src/main.ts`). What was not tested: any behaviour in a running Electron (what `senderFrame` holds for the window, and that it can be `null`: Read, typings, `senderFrame` is `WebFrameMain | null`, so a `null` sender MUST be refused). Provenance: Security review finding (M4), 2026-10-09; owner decision to fix, 2026-10-09. [US-2]

specs/features/home-network-sharing/tasks.md:266:**Done when:** tests first, then green. Pure function: `localhost:<port>`, `127.0.0.1:<port>`, `LOCALHOST:<port>`, each fixture interface address with the port, and `<hostname>.local:<port>` (also when the fixture hostname already ends in `.local`) are accepted; `evil.example:<port>`, a bare name with no port, `localhost:<other port>`, `[::1]:<port>`, `localhost.:<port>`, `localhost:<port>@evil.example`, an empty or missing header, and an address that is not on the fixture's interfaces are refused; a changed `interfaces` input changes the answer with no restart (an address present on the second call and absent on the first is accepted only on the second); an interface entry that is IPv6 or `internal` loopback contributes nothing beyond the fixed `127.0.0.1`. Gate, with sharing on and the machine injected: the rebinding case (`Host` and `Origin` both `evil.example:<port>`, which passed `isSameOrigin`, security review L1) is refused with `x-getwrite-gate: host-not-allowed` on `GET /pair`, on `POST /api/sharing/pair`, and on an API route (`/api/projects`), and on `/_next/static/x`; the same requests with an allowed `Host` are not refused for host reasons (they reach the credential refusal or the pairing exception as before); each interface address and each loopback name is accepted; the wrong port is refused; a request carrying the window secret is classified `window` and is NOT subject to the allowlist (the window works with any `Host` it sends); with sharing off nothing changes (pass-through, no allowlist). The pair route itself refuses a foreign `Host` without calling `verifyAndConsume` (spy). Existing gate tests built requests with `host: "10.0.0.5:3000"` or `localhost:3000`; they must keep testing what they test, so give them an injected machine whose interfaces include `10.0.0.5` (do not weaken any assertion; if an existing test changes only to add the machine, say which). The enumeration test sets an allowed `Host` for every request so that its refusals are credential refusals, and adds one case per credential kind with a foreign `Host` that must be refused with the host header. Contract: the frontend test reads `host-allowlist.cases.json` and checks `accepted` and `refused`; `electron/tests/addresses.test.ts` reads the same file and checks that `candidateAddresses` returns exactly `displayed` and that every displayed host appears in `accepted` (FR-31: the display and the gate follow one rule; a displayed address the gate refuses fails the test). Smoke script: add cases (node:http, since a `Host` override through `fetch` is not assumed to work) sending a forged `Host`/`Origin` of `evil.example:<port>` to `/pair`, `POST /api/sharing/pair` and `/api/auth-status`, expecting the host refusal; run it once against a fresh `pnpm build` (outside the sandbox if needed), exit code captured, output pasted in Notes. From `frontend/`: `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/sharing-host-allowlist.test.ts tests/unit/sharing-gate-proxy.test.ts tests/unit/sharing-pair-route.test.ts tests/unit/sharing-gate-enumeration.test.ts`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`; from `electron/`: `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`. The native export baseline check holds (the allowlist uses `node:os` inside `frontend/src/lib/sharing/`, not imported from `app/` except `proxy.ts` and the pairing route, as before).
specs/features/home-network-sharing/tasks.md:296:**Done when:** tests first, then green. `isLocalOriginUrl` with local origin `http://localhost:3000` (and again with another port, so no test depends on the value, FR-25): `http://localhost:3000/` and `http://localhost:3000/some/path?x=1#y` are allowed; `http://localhost:3000@evil.example/`, `http://localhost:30001/`, `http://localhost:3000.evil.example/`, `https://localhost:3000/`, `http://127.0.0.1:3000/`, `http://evil.example/`, `file:///etc/passwd`, `about:blank`, `javascript:alert(1)`, the empty string and `not a url` are refused (the first two were evaluated in Node as passing the old prefix test; evaluated in Node, not in Electron). `isTrustedSender`: the local origin URL is trusted; `null`, `undefined`, an empty string, a foreign origin, `http://localhost:30001/` and the userinfo URL are refused. Text tests over `main.ts`: the `will-navigate` handler contains no `startsWith(`; each of the four sharing channels' handlers calls `assertTrustedSender`; `electron/tests/sharing-ipc-surface.test.ts` still passes (the preload surface is unchanged). The handlers' refusal is a thrown error so the bridge call rejects, which the Task 20 UI already shows as an explicit error. From `electron/`: `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`. Not tested, stated in Notes: any behaviour in a running Electron (that `event.senderFrame.url` for the window's own calls is the local origin; that `senderFrame` can be `null`, which the typings declare as `WebFrameMain | null` and which this task refuses); that check is Task 24 item 18.
specs/features/paired-device-limits.md:125:FR-3: The experiments file MUST restate, as context only, the observation recorded in PDM experiments, "Exercise in the desktop app (pipeline lead, 2026-10-09)", "Found while running it, outside this feature": in development mode the app's `next dev` server child showed `TCP *:3000` in `lsof`, and from the host a request to the host's own LAN address returned 200 with project data and no `x-getwrite-gate` header with sharing off; it was sent from the host, not a second device; whether `next dev` ignores the `HOSTNAME=127.0.0.1` the app passes was not separated from other explanations, and whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked. The measurement of FR-1 MUST additionally record what classification a `next dev` request from the LAN address receives with sharing off (read, not run: with `GETWRITE_SHARING` unset the gate classifies every request `off`, so under the decided rule such a caller is not subject to the limits). No fix for the listen address is in this feature's scope; the record MUST say the matter is tracked separately. [US-3]

It was sent from the host, not from a second device; it was not separated from other explanations; it was not checked whether it predates Feature 75. The measurement above is consistent with that observation on a different port and route but does not settle those points. This matter is tracked separately and is not fixed here.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:1019:1. Dev mode left processes behind. After `app.close()` and 5 s, four of the nine pids the script had recorded as descendants of the pid it started were still alive: two `node`, one `next-server (v16.2.6)` and one more `node`. The script sent SIGTERM to exactly those recorded pids; after 3 s none was alive, and the listener on 3000 was gone. In the packaged run all five recorded pids were gone 5 s after the close with no signal sent. The cause is not established. A hypothesis is that `main.ts` starts the dev server with `spawn("pnpm", ["dev"], { shell: true })` and `before-quit` calls `serverProcess.kill()`, which signals the shell; the experiment that would settle it is to repeat the dev run and compare the pid tree and signal handling of the `pnpm`/`next` processes with and without that kill, which was not run. This is the behaviour of the development path only. Not investigated further and not changed.

From `electron/`: `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`; from `frontend/`: `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the touched test files pass; `pnpm knip` at the repo root reports nothing new (the new bridge methods are used by Task 7; if knip flags them unused before Task 7 lands, record that and re-check in Task 7).
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2902:**Notes:** Needs Feature 75's device records and gate, so it cannot ship first. It is separable from Feature 75 because a writer can pair and use a device without it, but a writer who shares without it has no way to cut a device off from the desktop app; see the interim-exposure note in Feature 75. Release hold (owner decision, Gate 2, 2026-10-09): build order is not release order; Feature 75 may merge to `main`, but no release containing it goes out until this feature and Features 77 and 80 are merged (this feature has since merged; see the status record below). Where revocation is enforced is the part that matters: it has to be checked on every request, not only at pairing time, or a revoked device keeps working until it re-pairs. That is a requirement of FR-67 as written; whether the gate re-reads the store on each request or caches is a design choice for the feature spec and has not been looked at. Nothing here was run (as drafted). Status, 2026-10-10 (verified with git by the pipeline lead): MERGED to `main` and NOT RELEASED. Implemented on branch `feat/paired-device-management`, merged as PR #264, merge commit `44ae6ca3`; the branch tip merged was `2566b566`; merged 2026-10-10. This feature has no release of its own to hold: the hold is Feature 75's, on home-network sharing as a whole, and with 76 merged it now waits on Features 77 and 80 only. (Update, 2026-10-10: Feature 77 has since merged, so the hold now waits on Feature 80 only; remaining home-network features 78, 79 and 80, all Not started.) A paired device can still lock, unlock and delete projects until Feature 77. Feature spec `specs/features/paired-device-management.md`, task list `specs/features/paired-device-management/tasks.md` (15 tasks, all ticked), measurements `specs/features/paired-device-management/experiments.md`. Owner decisions: Gate 3 (2026-10-09), revoke deletes the device record, the desktop main process writes the credential store under the server's lock protocol, no revoke-all, no repair of a corrupt store from the UI. The Gate 4 answer that the corrupt-store message shows only when sharing is on or pending was SUPERSEDED on 2026-10-10 (OQ-17, Task 15): an unreadable store is flagged in every sharing state. Also 2026-10-10: the wording stays as built for now and the copy is reviewed in one pass once the related sharing features are in; the pending-restart wording question (feature spec OQ-18) is deferred to that pass and remains open. Verified, per the records: suites against main (frontend 6035 passed, 1 skipped at `2509dd19`; 6046 passed, 1 skipped reported by the implementing agent after Task 15, not re-run by the lead; desktop 322; CLI 144 in the main checkout; lint and knip unchanged from baseline); a real revoke against a built server was refused on the first request on all 88 gated routes and pages, with a second device unaffected; in two real browsers the revoked one reached the pairing screen 3.2 s after the revoke, text typed after the revoke was not saved (hence the revoke dialog's warning sentence, "Unsaved edits on that device will be lost."), and the cookie was replaced on re-pair; the real Electron window, driven by script in development mode and in an unpacked build with seeded devices and sharing off, showed the list, blank-rename refusal, rename, the revoke dialog and focus, revoke, the 5 s poll and the unreadable-store message; and, reported by the owner from their own unpacked build and Android phone, no output pasted, pairing, the phone appearing in the list, revoking the connected phone, and pairing again. Not verified: a rename surviving an app restart in the real app; revoke with sharing off against a really paired phone; the unreadable-store message with sharing on or pending in the real app (component tests only); Node 20 locally (none on the machine; the lead did not see the PR's CI result before the merge); the owner's sign-off of the working-copy strings. Known limits: revoke takes effect on the device's next request (an idle revoked browser made no request for 45 s and kept showing its page); no last-used column, no revoke-all; devices from the same browser share an automatic name. Deviations and incidents: the revoke dialog stays open until the revoke finishes (the task text said it closes on confirm); during the exercise the lead's script stopped the owner's running desktop app (it killed every listener on port 3000; the script was then changed to refuse to run when the port is in use); one exercise run was invalid because it loaded pages from the owner's running app instead of the new code. Found outside this feature, recorded and not fixed: in development mode the app's `next dev` server listened on all interfaces with sharing off and answered a request to the host's own LAN address with project data (sent from the host, not a second device; whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked).
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2911:**Notes:** Rewritten 2026-10-10 twice. Gate 1 (Owner decision, Gate 1, 2026-10-10 (the pipeline lead's recommendation, accepted by the owner: 'Take your recs')): the earlier text (one opt-in setting for project deletion, and lock, unlock, sharing and pairing fixed as refused) is superseded in part by five limits, each a switch, all on by default. Gate 2 (Owner decision, Gate 2, 2026-10-10 (the pipeline lead's recommendation, accepted by the owner: 'Your recs')): the breakdown shown to the owner had four features (77, 81, 82, 83) and five numbered decisions, and the owner accepted them; this entry is the result, and Features 81, 82 and 83 were removed from the list (they were added earlier the same day and never committed). The five decisions: (1) the two measurements (formerly Feature 81) are folded into this feature as its first tasks, followed by a stop: results are reported to the owner before dependent work starts, the same shape Feature 75 used. (2) The paired-device experience (formerly Feature 83: limited controls shown disabled with the reason, and the three failure-visibility fixes) is folded in; nothing misleading ships separately. (3) The `projectId` validation fix for the encryption route (formerly Feature 82), if the measurement shows the flaw is real, is inside the release hold; the mechanism that gives effect to that with the least process is the pipeline lead's choice and is labelled as such: it is a conditional task inside this feature's task list, built only if measurement (ii) shows a traversal value is accepted and closed with a recorded "not needed" otherwise, so there is no separate feature. It remains a defect independent of sharing (OQ-85 in the product spec), and the feature spec and task list should say so. (4) The paired browser learns whether it is a paired device and which limits are on through one new read route, gated like every route and added to the route enumeration tests. (5) The desktop window shows the limits in App Settings only. Why one feature: server enforcement, the limits store with its desktop switches, the read route, the passphrase-prompt suppression and the visible-refusal behaviours are kept together because none is safe or honest to ship without the others. Enforcement without the store has nothing to read; the store without enforcement is a switch that does nothing; enforcement without the read route and the prompt suppression leaves a limited phone still offered the passphrase prompt on a locked workspace (the Start page opens it automatically; measured for a plain browser in `specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md` section D), and a server refusal alone does not keep the passphrase off the unencrypted network, because the passphrase has been typed and sent by the time the server refuses; and enforcement without the disabled controls and visible refusals ships a default install (every limit on) in which a lock refusal is silent and a refused project deletion shows "Project deleted". Release hold: the hold on home-network sharing is on Features 77 and 80 being merged, as the owner set it. Because the paired-device experience and the conditional `projectId` task are inside this feature, they are inside the hold with no further condition added. No condition is added by the defaults: shipping with every limit on by default satisfies the hold as stated. Consequence of all five defaults being on: after upgrading, an already-paired device can no longer delete projects, lock, unlock, change encryption or purge until the writer switches that limit off at the desktop; Features 75 and 76 are merged and unreleased, so only the owner's own builds are affected. Hard ordering inside the feature: the two measurements come first and are followed by a stop; the limits hold only if measurement (i) shows the classification reaches a POST route handler in both the standalone build and `next dev`, with a forged client copy replaced; if it does not, the design here (enforcement at the route) changes and the owner is told before dependent work (OQ-85 in the product spec). A third observation is context only and is not in this feature's scope: on 2026-10-09 a `next dev` server under the desktop app was seen listening on `*:3000` with sharing off and answering a request to the host's LAN address with project data (`specs/features/paired-device-management/experiments.md`, "Exercise in the desktop app"); it is tracked separately and no fix is pulled in here. The test with two paired clients across an unlock and a lock, which Feature 75's Notes left to a later task list, belongs in this feature's task list. Because the unlock switch off sends the passphrase over plain HTTP, the warning copy is part of the feature, not polish; the other copy is for the single copy pass the owner deferred. Open points carried to the feature spec: where the limits file lives and how the server finds it. Nothing here was run. Status, 2026-10-10 (verified with git by the pipeline lead): MERGED to `main` and NOT RELEASED. PR #266, merge commit `fc730749`, branch `feat/paired-device-limits`, branch tip merged `8e157dd7`, merged 2026-10-10. With this merged, the release hold on home-network sharing waits on Feature 80 (port change) only; remaining home-network features 78, 79 and 80, all Not started. Feature spec `specs/features/paired-device-limits.md`, task list `specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md` (31 tasks, all ticked), measurements `specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md`. Built: five limits (delete a project, unlock, lock, change encryption, permanently delete from the Trash), each a desktop switch on by default, global, effective on the next request; creating the workspace passphrase from a paired device is always refused; enforcement is at the route, answering 403 with a limit key; a missing or unparseable classification header is refused for every limited action. Owner decisions, 2026-10-10: the limits and their defaults; the encryption `projectId` fix shipped separately ahead of this feature as PR #265; a missing or unparseable classification header is refused unconditionally, whatever the limits file says (the feature spec's FR-10 wording was aligned); the old Feature 75 test allow-list of files importing the sharing library was updated to the four limited route files plus the limits read route; step 7 of the manual check (creating the passphrase from the phone) was accepted on scripted coverage; the copy is reviewed later in one pass. Verified, per the records: a built-server script against both the standalone build and `next dev` (195 checks, 0 failed, in each of the final standalone and `next dev` runs; every limited action covered); Playwright Chromium real-browser checks (107 checks, 0 failed); two paired clients across an unlock and a lock (31 checks, 0 failed); the real Electron app driven by script in development mode and in an unpacked build (167 checks each, 167 passed); and, reported by the owner, not observed by the pipeline, a manual check on the owner's Mac with an Android phone as the paired browser (steps 1 to 6, 8 and 9 "seem to work", no per-step detail; the unlock-off confirmation dialog appeared and was accepted as is). Suites: frontend 6434 passed, 1 skipped, reported by the implementing agent at the last change and not re-run by the lead; desktop 384; CLI 144; sharing smoke 91 enumerated entries, 28 checks, 0 failures; e2e 214 passed, 1 skipped, run locally by the lead after the Storybook fix. Defects found and fixed in the run: the pairing page reloaded itself for an unpaired device (found at the owner's manual check; 792 main-frame navigations in 8 s before the fix, 2 after, measured by the lead in Playwright; what the 2 are was not separated); a blank Start page when the limits read failed; revision-refusal wording that was inaccurate; dialog text with no colour class, black on the dark chrome background (measured: unclassed text under `<body>` is rgb(0, 0, 0) in both themes; which element the owner saw was not established); and Storybook had no limits provider, after which the e2e check on the PR failed. The failing e2e names were not seen, so the Storybook cause is inferred, and the lead did not see the CI result of the re-run before the merge (the owner reported afterwards, 2026-10-10, that CI passed). Not verified: Node 20 locally (none on the machine); creating the passphrase from the real phone (the owner's workspace already had one); the blank-Start-page, revision-wording and dialog-colour fixes re-measured in a browser; the rendered dark-mode dialogs; the sharing switch with restart in the scripted desktop runs. Known limits: an open page does not update on its own when the desktop locks or unlocks (7 s measured without an update); after a lock the paired browser's editor showed "Autosave failed", and the failing request was the save (PATCH of the revision, 404 `Revision ... not found`), cause not established, tracked separately. Deviations and incidents: the e2e suite was not part of the pipeline's final suites; no scripted check loaded the pairing page unpaired (their devices were paired by a helper or by a POST from Node), which is why none showed the reload; the passphrase gate sits in the Start page component, not in the page file. Found outside this feature, filed as notes and not fixed: soft-deleting a folder that contains folders returned HTTP 404 in the built-server script on both builds (not run on `main`, cause not established; the script's folder check therefore uses a leaf folder); `ConfirmDialog` details have no default text colour, so details text of other callers with no colour class would get the same black (callers not audited).
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2932:**Value:** The desktop app's bundled server stops using port 3000, a common default that other local software also uses, and uses a port chosen for GetWrite instead, so that a writer who shares projects on the home network (Feature 75) pairs devices against an address that is not a generic default.
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2933:**Vertical slice:** Logic: the port the desktop main process starts the bundled server on, and everything derived from it, changes from 3000 to a new value. Read, not run: the port is the constant `PORT` in `electron/src/server-config.ts` (line 11, `export const PORT = 3000;`; it moved there from `electron/src/main.ts`, which now imports it), and the window's own navigation guard and load URLs are built from it through `localOrigin(PORT)` in `electron/src/main.ts` (lines 292, 300, 304, 683, 732, 738, 745 and 782; the server's `PORT` env at line 173 and the sharing status at line 313); `BETTER_AUTH_URL` and the CSRF allowed-origin fallbacks default to `http://localhost:3000` in `frontend/src/lib/auth/auth-server.ts` and `frontend/app/api/_tenant/with-storage-context.ts`. Data: none unless the value is made configurable. Interface: none beyond the desktop window loading from the new address.
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2938:**Notes:** Raised by the owner at Gate 2, 2026-10-09: "I've been wanting to change it from 3000 (due to its default nature), that step may need to precede this work, and I don't know what address I want to use, but other Saboteur apps will be offset from it". Not decided, open points inside this feature: the port value itself (the owner has not chosen it; other Saboteur apps' ports will be offset from whatever GetWrite's is), and whether it is a fixed constant or configurable. Ordering (owner decision, 2026-10-09: "Port change proposal is fine, 80 before release"): Feature 75 is specified against a single port source and does not depend on its value, so Feature 80 need not precede 75's build; but Feature 80 MUST be merged before any release containing Feature 75, because a paired device saves the desktop's address (host and port), so changing the port after writers have paired devices would break their saved addresses. Together with the Gate 2 decision, the release containing Feature 75 is held until Features 76, 77 and 80 are all merged (76 has since merged, 2026-10-10). Nothing here was run. (Update, 2026-10-10: Features 76 and 77 have since merged, so the release hold waits on this feature only.) Owner decision, Gate 0, 2026-10-10: the desktop app's bundled server port becomes 7340 (was 3000), and Saboteur apps take blocks of ten: GetWrite owns 7340 to 7349, the next Saboteur app starts at base plus 10 (7350), and so on. This settles the port value above; whether the port is a fixed constant or configurable is still open. Screening done by the pipeline lead, stated as facts and not as guarantees: on the owner's Mac, `/etc/services` (a local copy of the registry, not the live IANA list) registers no TCP service in 7340 to 7349, 7350 to 7359, 7370 to 7379 or 7380 to 7389, while 7365 is registered in the 7360 block and 7391 to 7395 and 7397 in the 7390 block; 7340 is not among the common development defaults (3000, 4200, 5000, 5173, 6006, 7000, 8000, 8080, 8888, 9000) and lies between 1024 and 32767. That 7340 is absent from Chrome's and Firefox's restricted-port lists is from memory and was not checked against the browsers' current lists; the feature spec requires loading the app in real browsers to verify it. Feature spec: `specs/features/desktop-server-port.md`. The final suites for this feature include `pnpm test:e2e` alongside the unit suites, typecheck, lint, knip, the CLI suite and `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` (owner decision, Gate 0, 2026-10-10). Standing decisions carried from Features 75 to 77: working-copy strings are reviewed in one later copy pass; the Android app as a client is later.
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:3540:- **Partly carried, partly a new feature: listen address and port.** Owner words, Gate 2, 2026-10-09: "As for the listen address-- I've been wanting to change it from 3000 (due to its default nature), that step may need to precede this work, and I don't know what address I want to use, but other Saboteur apps will be offset from it". Recorded: (1) the owner wants the desktop server's port changed away from 3000 because 3000 is a common default; the new value is not decided, and other Saboteur apps' ports will be offset from whatever GetWrite's is. This is now Feature 80 (Change the desktop server's default port, Not started, no dependency); the port value and whether it is a fixed constant or configurable are open points inside Feature 80, not settled. (2) Ordering, resolved (owner decision, 2026-10-09: "Port change proposal is fine, 80 before release"): the owner said the port change "may need to precede this work". Feature 75 is specified against a single port source and does not depend on its value, so Feature 80 need not precede 75's build; but Feature 80 MUST be merged before any release containing Feature 75, because a paired device saves the desktop's address (host and port), so changing the port after writers have paired devices would break their saved addresses. With the earlier Gate 2 decision, the release containing Feature 75 is held until Features 76, 77 and 80 are all merged. Still open, inside Feature 80: the port value, and fixed constant versus configurable. (3) Carried to the Feature 75 spec: which interface(s) to listen on (all interfaces, or one chosen LAN interface), and what the address display shows on a machine with several addresses.

specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:90:FR-2: The existing guard tests MUST still pass unchanged: `electron/tests/server-config.test.ts` (exactly one numeric export, `PORT`; `main.ts` assigns no numeric literal to a port; read, not run). specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:92:FR-3: Each consumer of the desktop port MUST keep reading it from the single source, and a unit test MUST show it with a port other than 3000 or 7340 passed in as input. The consumers, read, not run: the server's `PORT` env (`electron/src/main.ts` line 173); the window load, retry and readiness URLs (lines 732, 738, 745); the navigation guard (line 683); the IPC sender checks (lines 292, 300, 304); the window-secret installation (line 782); the sharing status port (line 313) and `candidateAddresses(interfaces, port)` in `electron/src/sharing/addresses.ts`. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:94:FR-4: The server's own port MUST continue to come from its `PORT` env, which the main process sets, and not from a constant or the request. (Read, not run: `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts` line 158 calls `parseServerPort(env.PORT)` and refuses every request when it is missing or not a plain decimal in 1..65535; `host-allowlist.ts` builds accepted names as `<name>:<port>`; `same-origin.ts` compares `Origin`/`Referer` to `Host` and has no port constant.) A test MUST show a Host of `<lan-ip>:<PORT>` accepted and the same name on another port refused, with `PORT=7340`. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:96:FR-5: The task list MUST begin with a recorded search for 3000 used as a port, in a new `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md`: the exact command, its full output, and a classification of every hit as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal (for example `next dev` and `next start` with no `PORT`, which default to 3000: Hypothesis from Next's documented behaviour, to be settled by reading the dev scripts and by FR-12's run), (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, a word count, a test count). The search MUST cover source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`, and MUST also look for ports written without the literal. The list in this spec below is from a search made while writing it and is a starting point, not the record. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:106:FR-10: Port in use, today's behaviour, by reading first. The task MUST record what the code does when something already listens on the app's port, and then measure it (FR-22). Read, not run: the server is forked with `PORT` in its env (`main.ts` lines 173, 209); readiness (`electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts`, lines 49 to 53) polls the local origin every 500 ms for 30 s and resolves on any status below 500, sending the window secret header on each poll, with no check that the responder is the child it forked; a child exit raises the "Server exited unexpectedly" screen (`main.ts` line 815 and following); `requestSingleInstanceLock` hands focus to a running instance and quits the duplicate. Hypothesis, to be tested by FR-22: a foreign listener answering below 500 makes readiness succeed and the window load that listener's response, while the real server's bind fails. [US-4]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:110:FR-12: Development mode MUST follow OQ-2, whose direction is provisional (accepted by the owner for writing the task list and brought back at the FR-23 stop): `pnpm electron:dev` follows the constant (it already inherits `PORT`) and plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; a separate development port (7341) is an optional task the stop can turn on. Read, not run: `pnpm electron:dev` runs `electron dist/main.js`, whose `startServer` spawns `pnpm dev` in `frontend/` with the same env including `PORT` (`main.ts` lines 224 to 228), and `cli/src/qa/server.ts` documents that `next dev` honours `PORT`; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` run `next dev` with no port argument (`package.json`, `frontend/package.json` line 6). The disposition of each of `pnpm dev`, `pnpm start`, `pnpm electron:dev` and the demo scripts MUST be recorded, and a run MUST show which port a started dev server actually listens on. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:120:FR-17: With sharing on, the Host allowlist MUST accept `<lan-ip>:7340`, `localhost:7340` and `127.0.0.1:7340` and MUST refuse `<lan-ip>:3000` and `localhost:3000`, shown against a built server started on a free port set through `PORT` and, separately, against the real app. A request sent from the host to its own LAN address is not a second-device result and MUST be labelled so. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:144:FR-29: The final suites MUST be run against the FR-9 baselines and reported with counts: frontend `test:ci`, electron, CLI, typecheck for frontend and electron, lint, knip, `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` against a built standalone server (read, not run: `frontend/scripts/sharing-refusal-smoke.mjs` picks a free port itself and sets `PORT`, so it should not depend on 7340; to be shown), and `pnpm test:e2e` against Storybook on 6006. Any difference from baseline MUST be listed with its cause as measured or as unestablished. [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:156:- OQ-2 (provisional, open until the FR-23 stop): Does development mode move to 7340, stay on 3000, or take its own port in the block, and should a packaged app and a dev app be able to run at once? Evidence: read, not run, `electron:dev` already passes `PORT` to `next dev`, so it follows the constant; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` do not and default to 3000; the demo scripts and `demo/README.md` assume 3000; the QA harness picks a free port and is unaffected; whether the packaged and dev apps share a single-instance lock was not established. Options: (a) `electron:dev` follows the constant, `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; (b) all of them move to 7340 (`next dev -p`), so only one runs at a time; (c) dev takes a second port in the block (7341) so both run at once, with the demo scripts following; (d) all stay on 3000 and `electron:dev` is given its own override. Trade-off: (a) leaves the 3000 collision the owner wants to avoid in the everyday dev command; (b) removes it and forbids running both; (c) allows both and spends a number in the block. Provisional direction (accepted by the owner for writing the task list, to be brought back at the FR-23 stop): (a), with (c) an optional task the stop can turn on depending on whether a dev app and a packaged app can run at once (single-instance lock keying is unverified). — Impact: FR-12, FR-16, FR-24.

Every hit is classified as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal, (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, word count, test count), and ends with a disposition (follows the source, changed, deliberately left, or historical) and a reason. specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:153:FR-25: The server port MUST have a single source that the server's bind, the desktop window's load URL and navigation guard, and the displayed address all read. No requirement or test in this feature MAY depend on the port having any particular value. (Read: `electron/src/main.ts` today holds `PORT = 3000` and the load URL and `will-navigate` guard use `http://localhost:${PORT}`; choosing a new value is Feature 80.) [US-1]
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:173:FR-35: The window MUST be navigable only to the local origin: a navigation MUST be allowed only when the parsed URL's origin equals the local origin exactly. The sharing IPC handlers (`getwrite:sharing-get-status`, `-set-enabled`, `-generate-code`, `-get-code`) MUST refuse a call whose sender frame's origin is not the local origin. Evaluated in Node, not tested in Electron: the current `will-navigate` handler uses `url.startsWith(localOrigin(PORT))`, and `http://localhost:3000@evil.example/` and `http://localhost:30001/` both pass that prefix test while their parsed origins are `http://evil.example` and `http://localhost:30001`; the prefix test predates this branch. The bridge on this branch exposes `generatePairingCode`, `getPairingCode`, `setSharingEnabled` and `restart`, and the IPC handlers do not check the sender (Read, `electron/src/main.ts`). What was not tested: any behaviour in a running Electron (what `senderFrame` holds for the window, and that it can be `null`: Read, typings, `senderFrame` is `WebFrameMain | null`, so a `null` sender MUST be refused). Provenance: Security review finding (M4), 2026-10-09; owner decision to fix, 2026-10-09. [US-2]

Also not tested: whether the window's `http://localhost:${PORT}` load URL (Read, `main.ts` lines ~578-604) stays correct under a non-loopback bind, and whether `localhost` can resolve to the IPv6 loopback.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:778:`scripts/verify-limits.mjs <standalone|dev>` refuses to run if anything listens on port 3000 (the refusal branch itself was not exercised: nothing listened, and no listener was created to test it; read, not run). It creates a throwaway root under `os.tmpdir()` (its real path is asserted to be inside the real temp directory and outside `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron`, which the script never reads or writes), with its own `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` and `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`, a random per-run passphrase held in memory only, and a throwaway encrypted project created through the window. It starts server A (sharing on, window secret) in its own process group on a free loopback port (`next dev` is given `-H 127.0.0.1`; the Task 1 script did not pass that flag), pairs one device by writing `pairing-state.json` and POSTing the code (cookie held in memory), runs items (a) to (d), (f), (g), (h) on it, stops it, then starts server B (sharing off, same throwaway folders, the limits file all five on in its sharing directory) and runs (e). Every change to the limits file goes through the main-side writer `setLimit` from `electron/dist/sharing/limits-store.js` (no deviation). The only other file states are two deliberately unreadable ones that no writer produces: a mode-0 file whose contents say all five are off, and a directory at the file path. Fixtures (projects, resources, revisions, trashed items) are created by the window caller, which is never limited. The script prints request labels, status codes, the gate header value and `limit` keys; it prints no cookie, secret, passphrase or response text. After the run it checked the process groups it recorded (`ps -A -o pid=,pgid=`) and removed the root.
