#### Appendix 6. Command 8, next dev / next start / next-server / server.js / next build (231)

~~~~text
CLAUDE.md:48:pnpm build      # next build
CLAUDE.md:223:- Build flow: `pnpm electron:build` (frontend `next build` → electron `tsc` → electron `build:worker`); package with `pnpm electron:package` (electron-builder).
CLAUDE.md:251:- The app build lives on the frontend side: `frontend`'s `pnpm build:native` (`GETWRITE_BUILD_TARGET=native`, `frontend/scripts/build-native-static.mjs`) assembles a generated native app tree — a copy of `app/` with `app/api/**` and the hosted-auth pages (`app/login`, `app/reset-password`, `app/verify-email`) removed — in a shadow root (`frontend/.native-build/`, never mutating the real `frontend/app/`), then runs `next build` from there with `output: "export"` (`frontend/next.config.mjs`, gated on `GETWRITE_BUILD_TARGET === "native"`; `standalone` otherwise). The export lands directly at `frontend/out/`. `frontend/app/(app)/layout.tsx` short-circuits the hosted-auth redirect gate on native. `frontend/.browserslistrc` pins Chrome >= 67, the effective WebView JS-parsing floor.
CLAUDE.md:254:- CI: `.github/workflows/build-android.yml` runs that placeholder build on every push/PR to `main` as a non-launch build-health check; it does not exercise `frontend`'s `build:native` or `next build --output export`.
CLAUDE.md:293:- **Paired-device limits** (Feature 77) — Five desktop-side switches, in App Settings' "Home network sharing" section, that stop a paired device doing certain things: project deletion, unlocking (resume follows it), locking, encryption changes (enable with an existing key, plaintext export) and permanent deletion (Trash purge and Empty trash, revision delete). **Status: merged to `main` (PR #266, merge commit `fc730749`, 2026-10-10); not released.** Each is on (restricted) by default, global to every paired device (not per device), and effective on the next request with no restart; the desktop window is never limited, and sharing-off is never limited. Creating the workspace passphrase from a paired device is always refused, whatever the switches say. There are no switches for sharing, pairing or device management, which are desktop-only IPC with no HTTP route already. State is `userData/sharing-limits.json` (`{ version: 1, limits }`, `true` = restricted); a missing or unreadable file means all five on, and a missing or unparseable classification header is refused for every limited action. A refusal is HTTP 403 `{ error, limit }`; paired-device UI disables the control and shows a visible reason, and a refused lock, resume, Trash purge or revision delete shows a line. Switching Limit unlocking off asks for confirmation and shows three statements (the passphrase is typed on the paired device, sent over unencrypted HTTP, and a recorded one also unlocks files at rest). Distinct from Home-network sharing's gate (which decides who may reach the server at all) and from Feature 76's device management. Measured: built servers (standalone and `next dev`, 195 checks, script from the host machine), Playwright Chromium (107 checks), two scripted paired clients (31 checks), and the real Electron app in development mode and as an unpacked build (167 checks each, scripted, with sharing seeded on, not switched). Known limits and not verified: an already-open page on a paired device does not change on its own when the desktop or another client locks or unlocks (no change within 7 s without a reload; the next request and a reload show it); with the limits read failing, a paired browser on a locked workspace showed neither the passphrase prompt nor the "unlock at the desktop" message when measured (Playwright), and a later commit (`ad25f1bd`) now shows "This workspace is locked. This page could not check whether unlocking is allowed here." with a "Try again" button (hook value `didLastReadFail`; component tests only, not re-measured in a browser); a refused revision delete now says "Deleting a revision is turned off here. Do it at the desktop." (`REVISION_DELETE_REFUSAL_MESSAGE`; `checkLimit`/`refusalFor` take an optional message; the limit key is still `permanentDeletion`); the pairing page was measured reloading continuously for an unpaired device (792 navigations in 8 s) and, after the fix (`DeviceNotPairedGuard` does not navigate on `/pair`, `SharingLimitsProvider` makes no read there, `components/Sharing/pairing-page.ts`), 2 navigations in 8 s with 0 API requests on a throwaway sharing-on server — pairing in a browser after the fix was not scripted; dialog text added by this feature now carries `text-gw-secondary` (text with no colour class under `<body>` measured black in both themes; the rendered dialogs were not opened in a dark window and other `ConfirmDialog` callers' details text was not audited); after another paired client locked the workspace (Limit unlocking and Limit locking off), a paired browser's open editor showed "Autosave failed" and the failing request measured was the save (`PATCH`, 404), cause not established, tracked separately; Node 20 not run; the sharing on/off switch with its restart was not driven in the desktop runs (sharing was seeded on); the dev-mode desktop run was from a copied tree, not the repo tree; hosted auth not run; the owner's manual check with an Android phone (Task 31) was done and reported by the owner on 2026-10-10 — steps reported as "seem to work" with no per-step detail, step 7 (creating the workspace passphrase from the phone) not done because the owner's workspace already has a passphrase (scripted coverage only), no observation about the phone's open editor; the working-copy strings are unreviewed. Storybook had no limits provider, so the six components that read the limits state would throw in stories; `frontend/.storybook/preview.tsx` now wraps every story in `StaticSharingLimitsProvider` (default exempt; per-story `parameters.sharingLimits` override). CI e2e failed on the PR before this; the failing names were not seen, so that this was the cause is inferred; `pnpm test:e2e` run locally after the change: 214 passed, 1 skipped; the CI re-run result was not seen by the pipeline before the merge; the owner reported afterwards that CI passed. The e2e suite was not part of the pipeline's final suites for this feature. See `docs/features/home-network-sharing.md`.

specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:96:FR-5: The task list MUST begin with a recorded search for 3000 used as a port, in a new `specs/features/desktop-server-port/experiments.md`: the exact command, its full output, and a classification of every hit as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal (for example `next dev` and `next start` with no `PORT`, which default to 3000: Hypothesis from Next's documented behaviour, to be settled by reading the dev scripts and by FR-12's run), (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, a word count, a test count). The search MUST cover source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`, and MUST also look for ports written without the literal. The list in this spec below is from a search made while writing it and is a starting point, not the record. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:110:FR-12: Development mode MUST follow OQ-2, whose direction is provisional (accepted by the owner for writing the task list and brought back at the FR-23 stop): `pnpm electron:dev` follows the constant (it already inherits `PORT`) and plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; a separate development port (7341) is an optional task the stop can turn on. Read, not run: `pnpm electron:dev` runs `electron dist/main.js`, whose `startServer` spawns `pnpm dev` in `frontend/` with the same env including `PORT` (`main.ts` lines 224 to 228), and `cli/src/qa/server.ts` documents that `next dev` honours `PORT`; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` run `next dev` with no port argument (`package.json`, `frontend/package.json` line 6). The disposition of each of `pnpm dev`, `pnpm start`, `pnpm electron:dev` and the demo scripts MUST be recorded, and a run MUST show which port a started dev server actually listens on. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:118:FR-16: A device that opens the old address MUST be examined, not assumed: with nothing listening on 3000 (checked first), the record MUST state what a real Chromium and, by the owner, the Android phone's browser show. If a development server is also running on 3000 (OQ-2), the record MUST state whether it answers a request from the host's LAN address with project data, because the record of Feature 76 shows a `next dev` server did so with sharing off (measured then, from the host, not a second device; whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked). What is done about it follows OQ-5, whose direction is provisional: a release-note or docs line only, no redirector or listener on 3000, and text in the sharing panel only if this record shows the failure is confusing. [US-3]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:156:- OQ-2 (provisional, open until the FR-23 stop): Does development mode move to 7340, stay on 3000, or take its own port in the block, and should a packaged app and a dev app be able to run at once? Evidence: read, not run, `electron:dev` already passes `PORT` to `next dev`, so it follows the constant; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` do not and default to 3000; the demo scripts and `demo/README.md` assume 3000; the QA harness picks a free port and is unaffected; whether the packaged and dev apps share a single-instance lock was not established. Options: (a) `electron:dev` follows the constant, `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; (b) all of them move to 7340 (`next dev -p`), so only one runs at a time; (c) dev takes a second port in the block (7341) so both run at once, with the demo scripts following; (d) all stay on 3000 and `electron:dev` is given its own override. Trade-off: (a) leaves the 3000 collision the owner wants to avoid in the everyday dev command; (b) removes it and forbids running both; (c) allows both and spends a number in the block. Provisional direction (accepted by the owner for writing the task list, to be brought back at the FR-23 stop): (a), with (c) an optional task the stop can turn on depending on whether a dev app and a packaged app can run at once (single-instance lock keying is unverified). — Impact: FR-12, FR-16, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:188:- The `next dev` server listening beyond loopback with sharing off, found during Feature 76 and tracked separately; FR-16 only records whether it matters to the old address.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:12:- **Builds do NOT work in git worktrees on this machine** (measured earlier; cause not established). Every task that needs `pnpm build`, a standalone server, `next dev`, `pnpm test:sharing-smoke`, a browser, Storybook or the real Electron app runs DIRECTLY IN THE MAIN CHECKOUT, one such task at a time, never two at once; the orchestrator commits each before the next starts. Pure code tasks (8 to 14) need no build and may run in a worktree. Electron, Playwright, builds, Storybook and `next dev` run OUTSIDE the command sandbox (they fail inside it with `EMFILE` or `Operation not permitted`); every record says which was used.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:38:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records the exact commands (for example `rg -n -w '3000'` with the excluded directories named, plus searches for `localhost:3000`, `127.0.0.1:3000`, `:3000`, `PORT`, `-p 3000`, `--port`, `process.env.PORT`, `next dev`, `next start`, and ports written without the literal such as `3e3` or a port computed from another value) and their FULL output, covering source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`. Every hit is classified as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal, (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, word count, test count), and ends with a disposition (follows the source, changed, deliberately left, or historical) and a reason. The starting list of FR-6 is checked against the result and every difference is listed, including any hit the list missed. The expected dispositions per the Gate 3 answers are recorded as owner decisions: hosted-auth fallbacks and their two tests left; the `paired-device-limits` scripts left (historical, not valid against the new port, and why); the AppImage smoke workflow left except for a comment (Task 11). Every "changed" disposition names the test or check that will cover it (FR-24). `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:41:**Notes:** Satisfies FR-5, FR-6, FR-8, FR-24. No process is started. Classification (c) for `next dev` and `next start` is a Hypothesis from Next's documented behaviour until Task 4 shows the listening port.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:58:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records, per command, the port it LISTENS on measured by `lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -a -p <pid>` for the pid the task started: `pnpm dev` (frontend), `pnpm start` (alias for the frontend dev), and `pnpm electron:dev` in the copied-tree approach on today's code (3000); plus `next dev` with `PORT` set to a free port the task picks (to show `PORT` is honoured; the QA harness comment is read, this is the run); the demo scripts are read for the port they assume and marked "read, not run". For coexistence it records, by reading and by a run: how `requestSingleInstanceLock` is keyed (Electron keys it by `userData` path; stated as read from the Electron documentation if not run), and the result of starting two instances of the dev-mode copied-tree app with two DIFFERENT throwaway `--user-data-dir`s (the second quits or not; whether the second's server bound or failed), with the facts only. It states what is not established (for example the behaviour of a packaged build and a dev build together, which Task 19 can add to). All servers are started on 3000 only when the task has confirmed 3000 free, otherwise the task refuses; pids recorded and stopped by pid; throwaway folders removed. `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:78:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records: the check that 3000 and 7340 had no listener before the task started its own throwaway ones (the task refuses otherwise); with NOTHING on 3000, what Chromium (version recorded) shows for `http://<lan-ip>:3000` and `http://localhost:3000` (error page text, no secret); with a throwaway server on 3000 started by the task as a `next dev` of the repo's frontend in the copied tree with sharing off (pid recorded, stopped by pid), whether a request from the host to its own LAN address on 3000 returns project data or a refusal (status and whether `x-getwrite-gate` was present; labelled as from the host, not a second device; whether it predates Feature 75 is not checked); with a throwaway plain HTTP server the task starts on 7340 (pid recorded, stopped by pid), whether Chromium loaded `http://<lan-ip>:7340` and `http://localhost:7340` (accepted yes/no, with the Chromium version) and the page's status. Firefox, desktop Chrome and Safari are listed as "not checked", and the restricted-port statement in the spec's scope note stays labelled unchecked memory for them. The Android phone result is NOT claimed (Task 21). `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.

specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:111:FR-4: With sharing on, the server MUST listen on all interfaces (Owner decision, Gate 3, 2026-10-09, OQ-3). The accepted consequence is that the server is then also bound on non-LAN adapters (for example VPN and virtual adapters); the access gate (FR-8 to FR-12) is the control. A change to the sharing setting MUST take effect by restarting the server (the bind is fixed when the server starts). The desktop window MUST tell the writer that a restart is happening, and that message MUST be shown before the relaunch call is made, because no window exists after the app quits (Read, not run: `getwrite:restart` is `app.relaunch(); app.quit()`, `electron/src/main.ts` ~245-249). The window MUST NOT leave an unexplained blank window while it restarts. A sharing change and a workspace-folder change MUST share one restart (Recommended default from triage, approved by the owner at Gate 3, 2026-10-09 as part of approving the spec, OQ-9). Whether `next dev` (the unpackaged path) honours `HOSTNAME` is unverified; the experiment is to run it with a non-loopback `HOSTNAME` and connect from another device, and it is a task in the plan. [US-1]
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:161:FR-29: In sharing mode, every state-changing request (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) MUST pass a same-origin check that compares the request's `Origin` header (falling back to `Referer`) to the request's own `Host`, with no configured canonical URL; if both `Origin` and `Referer` are absent the request MUST be refused (fail closed) (Recommended default from triage, approved by the owner at Gate 3, 2026-10-09 as part of approving the spec, OQ-6). Which header a route handler sees as the original host is unverified (Read, not run: Next sets `x-forwarded-host ??= host`, `frontend/node_modules/next/dist/server/base-server.js:574`); the check MUST be written against the header the plan's design task has confirmed. Optional hardening, recorded as a suggestion and not a requirement: also require the `Host` to be a loopback address, a private-range IP or a `.local` name. [US-1]
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:179:- OQ-1 (experiment pending; To be settled by experiment (plan task), result reported to the owner before dependent work): How does the server tell the desktop app's own window from a paired or unpaired device? Impact: FR-12, FR-1, FR-8, FR-13, FR-26, FR-27, and Feature 77 (not buildable if the route code cannot make this distinction). Evidence so far (Read, not run): peer address alone is rejected as a mechanism, because `frontend/node_modules/next/dist/server/base-server.js:577` does `req.headers['x-forwarded-for'] ??= originalRequest.socket.remoteAddress`, so a client-supplied header is kept and a LAN device could claim 127.0.0.1. Keep a confirming experiment: send a forged `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1` from another device and log what a route sees; unverified whether the packaged standalone `server.js` runs the same code. Candidate: a per-launch random secret generated in the main process and passed to the server through its environment (main already sets the server env, `electron/src/main.ts` lines ~147-159), attached to the window's requests through `session.webRequest.onBeforeSendHeaders` and/or a cookie on the window's session (both exist in the Electron 34.5.8 typings; coverage of request kinds is undocumented). Experiment, in a packaged build: log which request kinds (navigation, fetch, `/_next` assets) carry the header and which carry the cookie. If the header misses some kinds, use the cookie. If neither covers every kind, stop and report; the named fallbacks, not chosen, are a thin launcher that overwrites the address header from the real socket, and a second loopback listener (a second process with its own module state, including the module-level keyring variable in `frontend/src/lib/models/crypto/keyring-session.ts`, so it risks splitting unlock state). Coupling to settle in the same task: the pairing code is displayed in the main process's window while the code-entry endpoint and the attempt counter live in the server process, so who generates and holds the code, and how the window obtains it, must be decided together with the mechanism.
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:181:- OQ-3 (resolved at Gate 3, 2026-10-09; see Resolved questions) Remaining unverified fact: whether `next dev` honours `HOSTNAME` (FR-4); experiment named there. Also not tested: whether the window's `http://localhost:${PORT}` load URL (Read, `main.ts` lines ~578-604) stays correct under a non-loopback bind, and whether `localhost` can resolve to the IPv6 loopback.
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:197:- OQ-3 (resolved): Which interface does the server listen on, and what does the address display show? Resolution: all interfaces; the desktop window shows every candidate (private-range / LAN) address and states "no network address found" (working copy) when there is none. Accepted consequence: the server is also bound on non-LAN adapters; the gate is the control. The `next dev` `HOSTNAME` sub-point stays an unverified fact with its experiment named (OQ-3a). Evidence: Owner decision, Gate 3, 2026-10-09, option (a). Impact: FR-4, FR-6, FR-25.
specs/features/home-network-sharing.md:219:What the review did NOT cover: the gate was not run end to end on a build of HEAD; Electron runtime behaviour; `next dev`; WebSocket upgrades.
specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md:24:- Standalone layout observed: `.next/standalone/frontend/` contained only `node_modules`, and `server.js` was at `.next/standalone/.claude/worktrees/agent-a89ab57bebddae337/frontend/server.js`, i.e. the output was nested under the path from the main checkout root. The Next warning above said the inferred workspace root was the main checkout's `pnpm-workspace.yaml`. This is the layout in this worktree; it is not the `standalone/frontend/server.js` layout `electron/src/main.ts` expects, which was therefore not reproduced here. Under that nested directory, `.next/server/` contained `middleware.js`, `middleware-build-manifest.js`, `middleware-manifest.json` and `middleware/`, `proxy.ts` was copied next to `server.js`, and `SPIKE_LOG` was present in the same chunk file name. Whether the packaged (`standalone/frontend`) layout behaves the same was not measured.
specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md:30:`SPIKE_LOG=$TMPDIR/spike1.log PORT=4817 HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0 node server.js`
specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md:77:Also from (3): a request with no `X-Forwarded-For` to `127.0.0.1` was seen as `127.0.0.1`. So in this build, with no header sent the server supplied the connection's address; with a header sent, the client's value reached both the proxy and the route unchanged. This matches the spec's "Read, not run" reading of `base-server.js:577` as to behaviour; the source line itself was not re-read in this task.
specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md:118:It builds `frontend/.native-build/` by iterating the top-level entries of `frontend/`. Skipped entirely: `.next`, `out`, `.native-build`. specs/features/home-network-sharing/tasks.md:196:**Done when:** tests first, then green. The enumeration test walks `frontend/app/api` for every `route.ts` and `frontend/app` for every `page.tsx` using `node:fs` (no hard-coded list), converts each path to a URL (route-group folders like `(app)` removed; dynamic segments such as `[project-id]` and catch-alls such as `[...all]` replaced by a fixed dummy value), and asserts a minimum count so an empty walk fails (state the counts observed in the test file's comment at the time of writing). For each enumerated URL and for each of the HTTP methods the route file exports (read the exported names `GET`, `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE` from the file text), it builds a `NextRequest` and runs `frontend/proxy.ts` with sharing on and each of six credential cases (no credential, a malformed cookie, an unknown credential, a credential valid for a different store directory, a corrupt store, and a request from loopback with no secret, sent both without a forwarding header and with `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1`, classified like any other unconfirmed client), and expects a refusal (401 or 403 or a redirect to `/pair`) for every case, except the documented exceptions (`/pair` page, `POST /api/sharing/pair`, and `/_next/static` per the Task 6 decision), which are listed in the test by name and counted (so adding an exception changes a number the test checks). It asserts the pairing page and route exist in the enumeration. A dedicated test for `/api/version-check` stubs `globalThis.fetch` with a spy and asserts that the refused unconfirmed request makes zero outbound calls, and that the same request from the window header reaches pass-through (FR-10). A test shows a route added to a temp copy of the tree is picked up automatically (point the walker at a temp directory). The smoke script builds nothing: it starts `frontend/.next/standalone/frontend/server.js` (built by the caller with `pnpm build`, static files copied as in Task 1) on a free loopback port with `GETWRITE_SHARING=1`, `GETWRITE_WINDOW_SECRET`, and `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR` pointing at a temp directory, then with `fetch` checks (the script runs from loopback, so its no-credential case is the loopback-without-secret case and is asserted as such): every enumerated URL refused with no credential, with a forged `X-Forwarded-For: 127.0.0.1`, and with a made-up cookie; the window header passes `/api/auth-status`; a pairing with a code written by the script (using the contract fixture format) yields a cookie that passes a GET; the same cookie fails a cross-origin POST; a corrupt store file refuses everything non-window; the script exits non-zero on any mismatch and never prints a code or secret. `pnpm exec vitest run tests/unit/sharing-gate-enumeration.test.ts` passes; the smoke script is run once against a fresh `pnpm build` (outside the sandbox if needed) and its output is pasted into this task's Notes; `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` pass.
specs/features/home-network-sharing/tasks.md:235:**Files:** `electron/src/sharing/sharing-mode.ts` (add `resolveServerHostname(mode)` returning `"0.0.0.0"` when `mode.effective` and `"127.0.0.1"` otherwise), `electron/src/main.ts` (`HOSTNAME` in `startServer`'s env becomes `resolveServerHostname(...)`; the call that computes the mode is the one Task 13 added; if sharing is blocked by hosted auth, log that sharing is not started and keep loopback), `electron/tests/sharing-mode.test.ts` (extend). FR-34 additions (the design below is this task's, from reading the code, and is not a spec decision): `electron/src/sharing/sharing-mode.ts` also exports `buildServerBindEnv(mode)` returning `{ HOSTNAME, GETWRITE_BIND, GETWRITE_SHARING }` from the one `mode`: `HOSTNAME` and `GETWRITE_BIND` both `resolveServerHostname(mode)`, and `GETWRITE_SHARING` `"1"` exactly when `mode.effective` else `"0"`; `electron/src/main.ts`'s `startServer` replaces its `HOSTNAME` and `GETWRITE_SHARING` entries with a spread of `buildServerBindEnv(mode)` (one `mode` computed once, Task 13's call), so no second expression can disagree; `frontend/src/lib/sharing/sharing-env.ts` adds `BIND_ENV = "GETWRITE_BIND"` and `bindBeyondLoopback(env)` (true when the variable is present, even empty, and its value is not exactly `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` or `::1`; absent means undeclared and is false, so `pnpm dev`, hosted deployments and tests that never set it are unchanged); `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts` and `classify-request.ts`: when `bindBeyondLoopback(env)` is true and `GETWRITE_SHARING` is not exactly `"1"`, a request carrying the correct window secret passes (the window must still work) and every other request, including `/pair`, `POST /api/sharing/pair` and `/_next/static`, is refused with a 403 JSON body and header `x-getwrite-gate: bind-without-gate` (never a redirect to `/pair`); `frontend/tests/unit/sharing-gate-proxy.test.ts` (extend), `frontend/tests/unit/sharing-pair-route.test.ts` (extend: the route refuses in this state too). Why this shape (reading): `server.js` in the local standalone build reads `process.env.HOSTNAME || '0.0.0.0'` (`frontend/.next/standalone/frontend/server.js` line 9, a generated file; confirm it in the current build), so the gate cannot treat an unset `HOSTNAME` as proof of anything without breaking `next dev` and hosted use; a separate variable that main alone sets from the same value is the only signal that exists only on the desktop path.

specs/features/paired-device-limits.md:121:FR-1: The first task MUST record, in a new experiments file `specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md`, measurement (i): what `x-getwrite-classification` a route handler receives on a POST with a JSON body, for each of four callers (the desktop window presenting the window secret; a paired device; an unpaired device; and a request with sharing off), in both the standalone build and `next dev`, including a request that carries a forged client copy of the header and a control GET. The measurement MUST use a throwaway route or a test route that echoes the header and MUST NOT call any limited action. (Read, not run: the gate removes any client copy and sets its own with `NextResponse.next({ request: { headers } })`, `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts`; no route or component reads the header today, a text search of `app`, `src`, `components`, `tests`, `scripts` and `proxy.ts` found it only in `gate.ts`, `classify-request.ts` and two unit tests. Measured: experiments section E, a GET in a standalone build with a throwaway proxy that always replaced the header; POST, pages, the packaged layout and `next dev` were not measured there.) [US-3]
specs/features/paired-device-limits.md:125:FR-3: The experiments file MUST restate, as context only, the observation recorded in PDM experiments, "Exercise in the desktop app (pipeline lead, 2026-10-09)", "Found while running it, outside this feature": in development mode the app's `next dev` server child showed `TCP *:3000` in `lsof`, and from the host a request to the host's own LAN address returned 200 with project data and no `x-getwrite-gate` header with sharing off; it was sent from the host, not a second device; whether `next dev` ignores the `HOSTNAME=127.0.0.1` the app passes was not separated from other explanations, and whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked. The measurement of FR-1 MUST additionally record what classification a `next dev` request from the LAN address receives with sharing off (read, not run: with `GETWRITE_SHARING` unset the gate classifies every request `off`, so under the decided rule such a caller is not subject to the limits). specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:778:`scripts/verify-limits.mjs <standalone|dev>` refuses to run if anything listens on port 3000 (the refusal branch itself was not exercised: nothing listened, and no listener was created to test it; read, not run). It creates a throwaway root under `os.tmpdir()` (its real path is asserted to be inside the real temp directory and outside `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron`, which the script never reads or writes), with its own `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` and `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`, a random per-run passphrase held in memory only, and a throwaway encrypted project created through the window. It starts server A (sharing on, window secret) in its own process group on a free loopback port (`next dev` is given `-H 127.0.0.1`; the Task 1 script did not pass that flag), pairs one device by writing `pairing-state.json` and POSTing the code (cookie held in memory), runs items (a) to (d), (f), (g), (h) on it, stops it, then starts server B (sharing off, same throwaway folders, the limits file all five on in its sharing directory) and runs (e). Every change to the limits file goes through the main-side writer `setLimit` from `electron/dist/sharing/limits-store.js` (no deviation). The only other file states are two deliberately unreadable ones that no writer produces: a mode-0 file whose contents say all five are off, and a directory at the file path. Fixtures (projects, resources, revisions, trashed items) are created by the window caller, which is never limited. The script prints request labels, status codes, the gate header value and `limit` keys; it prints no cookie, secret, passphrase or response text. After the run it checked the process groups it recorded (`ps -A -o pid=,pgid=`) and removed the root.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:810:2. Soft-deleting a folder that has child folders returned HTTP 404 `Folder not found` in every case tried, on both builds: `ENOENT: no such file or directory, rename '<projects>/<id>/folders/workspace/chapters/chapter-1' -> '<projects>/<id>/.trash/folders/chapter-1'`. Observations (OBS lines, not pass/fail checks), standalone and `next dev` alike: a paired device with all five on, first attempt: 404; the window on a fresh project, first attempt: 404; a paired device on a fresh project whose `.trash/folders` directory was created beforehand, first attempt: 404. So the 404 did not depend on the caller or on that directory's absence, and it carries no `limit` key and no gate header. A leaf folder soft-deletes with 200 for the paired device. This is a response of the existing folder soft-delete route on the `novel` project type's folder tree; the cause was not established (the experiment that would settle it is to repeat the call on the `main` branch's build and read `softDeleteFolder` against the `folders/` layout `createProjectFromType` writes). No product source was changed.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:967:- In `electron/`: `pnpm build` (exit 0, `tsc`) and `pnpm build:worker` (exit 0) were re-run. Then `pnpm exec electron-builder --config electron-builder.yml --dir`, which printed "skipped macOS application code signing" (no valid Developer ID identity; the one identity found has an expired certificate) and finished; I did not capture its exit status (my `PIPESTATUS` expression printed an empty value in this shell), but `dist-electron/mac-arm64/GetWrite.app` has a new modification time (17:41:51; the one it replaced was from 12:41 the same day, built before Feature 77 existed), `Contents/MacOS/GetWrite` is present, and `Contents/Resources/frontend/.next/standalone/frontend/server.js` is present with a `BUILD_ID` identical to `frontend/.next/BUILD_ID`. specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:975:- Development mode: `main.ts` takes `isPackaged === false` to mean "projects live in `<repo root>/projects`", with no override, and passes that to the server as `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` after spreading `process.env` (so the environment cannot override it). Running the real `electron/dist/main.js` in place would therefore have written an encrypted project and a keyring into the repository's own `projects/`. So the script builds a throwaway fake repo root: a copy of `electron/dist` and `electron/package.json`, symlinks `frontend` and `getwrite-config` to the real ones, and an empty `projects/`; Electron is started on `<throwaway>/repo/electron`. `__dirname` is then the fake root's `electron/dist`, `isPackaged` is false, and the server is `pnpm dev` (`next dev`) run in the real `frontend/` through the symlink. That means dev mode here is "the development code path in a copied tree", not `electron dist/main.js` in the repo tree itself; the difference was not measured.
specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md:1019:1. Dev mode left processes behind. After `app.close()` and 5 s, four of the nine pids the script had recorded as descendants of the pid it started were still alive: two `node`, one `next-server (v16.2.6)` and one more `node`. The script sent SIGTERM to exactly those recorded pids; after 3 s none was alive, and the listener on 3000 was gone. In the packaged run all five recorded pids were gone 5 s after the close with no signal sent. The cause is not established. A hypothesis is that `main.ts` starts the dev server with `spawn("pnpm", ["dev"], { shell: true })` and `before-quit` calls `serverProcess.kill()`, which signals the shell; the experiment that would settle it is to repeat the dev run and compare the pid tree and signal handling of the `pnpm`/`next` processes with and without that kill, which was not run. This is the behaviour of the development path only. Not investigated further and not changed.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md:12:- **Builds do NOT work in git worktrees on this machine** (measured in the previous feature; cause not established). Every task that needs `pnpm build`, a standalone server, `next dev`, `pnpm test:sharing-smoke`, a browser, or the real Electron app runs DIRECTLY IN THE MAIN CHECKOUT, one such task at a time; the orchestrator commits each before the next starts. specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md:25:**What:** Measure what `x-getwrite-classification` a route handler receives on a POST with a JSON body for the window, a paired device, an unpaired device and sharing off, including a forged client copy and a control GET, in both the standalone build and `next dev`, and record it in `specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md`.
specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md:27:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records, with date, commands and exact observed values: for each of the four callers (desktop window presenting the window secret; paired device with a cookie; unpaired device; sharing off) and each of GET and POST with a JSON body, the HTTP status and the header value the handler echoed, in the standalone build (`pnpm build`, then the standalone server) AND in `next dev`; and for each caller one POST carrying a forged client copy of the header (value `window`), what the handler saw. It states the verdict for FR-4 plainly: the header reaches a POST handler in each build (yes/no per build) and a forged copy survives (yes/no per build). It also records what classification a `next dev` request from the host's LAN address receives with sharing off, and restates, as context only, the Feature 76 observation of FR-3 (development-mode server child showed `TCP *:3000`; a request from the host to its own LAN address returned 200 with project data and no `x-getwrite-gate` header with sharing off; sent from the host, not a second device; not separated from other explanations; not checked whether it predates Feature 75) and says the matter is tracked separately. Only a window secret placeholder, never the real value, appears in the record. The script refuses to run when port 3000 has a listener, picks free ports, and stops only the pids it started. `git status --short` from the repo root afterwards shows no change outside `specs/` (the throwaway route deleted; paste the output).

specs/features/paired-device-management.md:155:FR-21: The list lives in its own component with its own heading inside the "Home network sharing" section of App Settings, and its device region MUST scroll when there are many devices (Recommended default from triage, approved by the owner at Gate 3, 2026-10-09 as part of approving the spec, OQ-10). The list and its actions MUST meet the accessibility standard (WCAG 2.1 AA, `docs/standards/accessibility.md`): the list is a semantic list or table with a heading; every action button's accessible name includes the device name (for example "Rename Safari on iPad", "Revoke Safari on iPad"), so two devices with the same automatic name are distinguishable by something other than position, which is why the paired date MUST be in the accessible name or description when names collide; everything is operable by keyboard; focus is returned to a sensible place after a revoke (the next device, or the list heading when none remain) and after a cancelled rename or dialog; the result of a rename or revoke and any error is announced through a polite live region or an `alert`; the rename field has a visible label; text meets AA contrast; and no state is conveyed by colour alone. An axe test in the pattern of `frontend/tests/a11y/sharing-settings.a11y.test.tsx` MUST cover the list in the states: empty, several devices, store unreadable, a rename error, and the revoke dialog open. The accessibility checks MUST include that focus returns to a sensible place when the nested confirmation dialog closes (OQ-10). [US-1] [US-2] [US-3]

Your other devices are not affected."; confirm "Revoke {name}"; cancel "Keep {name}" (the description gains "Unsaved edits on that device will be lost." only in Task 12, and only if Task 11 says so; this task does not add it). The dialog shows no red class (assert the rendered dialog's class names contain no red token; `ConfirmDialog` uses the `destructive` button variant, which the repo styles like `secondary`, and the test asserts the variant is used unchanged). The default focus when the dialog opens is the cancel button (assert `document.activeElement`; if `ConfirmDialog` does not do this by itself, the task finds the smallest change inside `PairedDevices.tsx` that does, and does not edit the shared dialog unless a test of the shared dialog shows that is needed, reported). Cancel closes the dialog, calls nothing on the bridge, and returns focus to that device's Revoke button. Confirm calls `revokePairedDevice(id)` exactly once, closes the dialog, re-reads the list from the bridge and shows what is stored; on `ok` it announces "{name} was revoked." (working copy) in a polite live region and moves focus to the next device's row control, or the previous one if it was last, or the list heading if no device remains (FR-21); on `not-found` it shows "This device was already removed." and refreshes; on `corrupt`, `lock-not-acquired` and `write-failed` it shows "Could not revoke this device. It is still paired." and the list is re-read and still shows the device if the store still holds it (use a stub whose store keeps the device); a double-click on the confirm button does not call revoke twice (disable while pending). Axe tests (all in the existing file, using `runAxe`) cover each state of FR-21: empty, several devices, store unreadable, a rename error, the revoke dialog open; and a test checks that after the nested confirmation dialog closes by cancel, by Escape, and by confirm, focus is inside the list region and not on `document.body`. From `frontend/`: the touched test files, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` pass; `pnpm knip` at the repo root reports nothing new.

specs/features/subtype-templates-duplicates/tasks.md:122:**Done when:** the pipeline lead, at the exercise stage, with `next dev` and a disposable projects directory (Playwright-driven or by hand), has recorded for each of three cases whether the typed text survived: (1) open a text resource, edit, use Duplicate in the resource tree, open the copy, type text, wait more than 8 seconds, reload, reopen the copy: the text is present on disk (`content.txt`, `content.tiptap.json`) and in the editor; (2) the same for a copy made by `getwrite-cli templates duplicate` and opened in the app; (3) a resource made by `getwrite-cli templates create` from a template and opened in the app: the project loads, the resource opens, and typed text survives a reload. specs/features/subtype-templates-duplicates/tasks.md:125:**Notes:** NOT executable by a non-interactive implementor. **The orchestrator MUST leave this task unticked and MUST NOT treat that as a failure; the final verification (Task 11) does not depend on it.** Satisfies FR-32 (manual half). Record what was observed and name no cause that the observation did not discriminate. If Task 5 reported that the editor test could not be combined with the real model functions, this step is the only end-to-end evidence for FR-32 and the documentation stage must say so. **Measured result (pipeline lead, 2026-10-08):** branch `feat/subtype-templates-duplicate` at 3cf3fd75; `next dev` on port 3112 started by the repo owner with a disposable `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR`; browser driven by Playwright; files read on disk after each step; CLI commands run by the repo owner outside the sandbox from the branch build. A novel-type project was created in the app and the subtype "Scene" added to its list. Case 1, UI duplicate: typed BASELINEEDIT into "Location Profile" (on disk in `content.txt` and `content.tiptap.json` within 5 s), set its subtype to Scene, used Duplicate in the resource tree. The copy had `revisions/<id>/v-1` and `resourceSubtype: Scene` on disk and showed Scene in the sidebar. Typed COPYEDIT into the copy; after 9 s it was in both of the copy's content files and not in the source's; after a page reload and reopening the copy the editor text ended "BASELINEEDIT COPYEDIT". Case 2, CLI duplicate: `templates duplicate` of "Location Profile" exited 0; the duplicate had `v-1`, subtype Scene, the source's name and folder; opened in the app it showed Scene; typed CLIDUPEDIT; after 9 s `content.txt` and the `v-1` revision both ended "BASELINEEDIT CLIDUPEDIT"; after a reload the editor showed the same. Case 3, template-created: `templates create` from a hand-written template (`resourceSubtype: Scene`, `userMetadata.status: Draft`, two-paragraph body) exited 0; the resource had the app layout, `v-1`, subtype Scene, `userMetadata {status: Draft}`, `folderId` null, `orderIndex` 4, and no flat file under `resources/`; the project loaded in the app with the resource in the tree; opened, it showed Scene, status Draft and the body; typed TPLEDIT, on disk after about 9 s and in the editor after a reload. Also: `templates save-from-resource` from the app-edited "Location Profile" exited 0 and wrote keys `id`, `name`, `plainText`, `resourceSubtype`, `type` (subtype Scene; body ending "BASELINEEDIT"; no `userMetadata` key, the source's being empty); `templates create` from it produced "Round Trip Scene" with subtype Scene and that body, which opened in the app, took the edit RTEDIT, and kept it across a reload. `templates list` printed two `<id>\t<name>\t<type>` lines. No toast or error was observed in any case. Not exercised: the menu's "Copy" action (Duplicate was used), image and audio copies, a template with a `folderId`, an invalid subtype, an encrypted project, and mentions after a CLI create (see Task 9). One false alarm during the check, recorded so it is not repeated: a substring match on the marker DUPEDIT also matched CLIDUPEDIT; reading the file tails showed each resource held only its own text.

specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2902:**Notes:** Needs Feature 75's device records and gate, so it cannot ship first. It is separable from Feature 75 because a writer can pair and use a device without it, but a writer who shares without it has no way to cut a device off from the desktop app; see the interim-exposure note in Feature 75. Release hold (owner decision, Gate 2, 2026-10-09): build order is not release order; Feature 75 may merge to `main`, but no release containing it goes out until this feature and Features 77 and 80 are merged (this feature has since merged; see the status record below). Where revocation is enforced is the part that matters: it has to be checked on every request, not only at pairing time, or a revoked device keeps working until it re-pairs. That is a requirement of FR-67 as written; whether the gate re-reads the store on each request or caches is a design choice for the feature spec and has not been looked at. Nothing here was run (as drafted). Status, 2026-10-10 (verified with git by the pipeline lead): MERGED to `main` and NOT RELEASED. Implemented on branch `feat/paired-device-management`, merged as PR #264, merge commit `44ae6ca3`; the branch tip merged was `2566b566`; merged 2026-10-10. This feature has no release of its own to hold: the hold is Feature 75's, on home-network sharing as a whole, and with 76 merged it now waits on Features 77 and 80 only. (Update, 2026-10-10: Feature 77 has since merged, so the hold now waits on Feature 80 only; remaining home-network features 78, 79 and 80, all Not started.) A paired device can still lock, unlock and delete projects until Feature 77. Feature spec `specs/features/paired-device-management.md`, task list `specs/features/paired-device-management/tasks.md` (15 tasks, all ticked), measurements `specs/features/paired-device-management/experiments.md`. Owner decisions: Gate 3 (2026-10-09), revoke deletes the device record, the desktop main process writes the credential store under the server's lock protocol, no revoke-all, no repair of a corrupt store from the UI. The Gate 4 answer that the corrupt-store message shows only when sharing is on or pending was SUPERSEDED on 2026-10-10 (OQ-17, Task 15): an unreadable store is flagged in every sharing state. Also 2026-10-10: the wording stays as built for now and the copy is reviewed in one pass once the related sharing features are in; the pending-restart wording question (feature spec OQ-18) is deferred to that pass and remains open. Verified, per the records: suites against main (frontend 6035 passed, 1 skipped at `2509dd19`; 6046 passed, 1 skipped reported by the implementing agent after Task 15, not re-run by the lead; desktop 322; CLI 144 in the main checkout; lint and knip unchanged from baseline); a real revoke against a built server was refused on the first request on all 88 gated routes and pages, with a second device unaffected; in two real browsers the revoked one reached the pairing screen 3.2 s after the revoke, text typed after the revoke was not saved (hence the revoke dialog's warning sentence, "Unsaved edits on that device will be lost."), and the cookie was replaced on re-pair; the real Electron window, driven by script in development mode and in an unpacked build with seeded devices and sharing off, showed the list, blank-rename refusal, rename, the revoke dialog and focus, revoke, the 5 s poll and the unreadable-store message; and, reported by the owner from their own unpacked build and Android phone, no output pasted, pairing, the phone appearing in the list, revoking the connected phone, and pairing again. Not verified: a rename surviving an app restart in the real app; revoke with sharing off against a really paired phone; the unreadable-store message with sharing on or pending in the real app (component tests only); Node 20 locally (none on the machine; the lead did not see the PR's CI result before the merge); the owner's sign-off of the working-copy strings. Known limits: revoke takes effect on the device's next request (an idle revoked browser made no request for 45 s and kept showing its page); no last-used column, no revoke-all; devices from the same browser share an automatic name. Deviations and incidents: the revoke dialog stays open until the revoke finishes (the task text said it closes on confirm); during the exercise the lead's script stopped the owner's running desktop app (it killed every listener on port 3000; the script was then changed to refuse to run when the port is in use); one exercise run was invalid because it loaded pages from the owner's running app instead of the new code. Found outside this feature, recorded and not fixed: in development mode the app's `next dev` server listened on all interfaces with sharing off and answered a request to the host's own LAN address with project data (sent from the host, not a second device; whether it predates Feature 75 was not checked).
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2906:**Vertical slice:** Start: two measurements, then a stop, before anything depends on them (the shape Feature 75 used, and the shape of Features 57 and 63): (i) against the standalone build and against `next dev`, a POST with a JSON body to a gated route, observing what `x-getwrite-classification` the handler receives for a desktop-window, a paired, an unpaired and a sharing-off caller, including a request carrying a forged copy of the header (Feature 75's experiments section E measured this for a GET only; the gate is `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts` and the header constant is in `frontend/src/lib/sharing/classify-request.ts`); (ii) `enable` and `export` sent to `frontend/app/api/encryption/route.ts` with a traversal `projectId`, against a throwaway workspace, observing whether it is rejected before a path is built (triage found no validation by reading, `frontend/app/api/encryption/route.ts` ~195-204 and `frontend/src/lib/models/crypto/enable-encryption.ts` ~118; not run). The results go in an experiments file under `specs/features/` and are reported to the owner before the dependent work starts. Data: a limits store, one set of five on/off settings for all paired devices, in a file of its own beside the other sharing files (never inside `device-credentials.json`, whose schema would make an older build read a newer file as corrupt; read, not run), where absent, unparseable or unreadable means all five on; where the file lives and how the server finds it is carried to the feature spec. Logic: (1) the main-side writer follows the shape Feature 76 gave `device-credentials.json` (`electron/src/sharing/device-store-writer.ts`, read-modify-write under the lock protocol, with `electron/src/sharing/device-ipc.ts` as the model for handlers whose first statement is the sender check, registered in `electron/src/main.ts` beside the `getwrite:sharing-list-devices` handlers and exposed through `electron/src/preload.ts` and `frontend/src/lib/desktop-bridge.ts`); (2) one shared check in `frontend/src/lib/sharing/` beside `classify-request.ts` that reads the classification the gate forwards in `CLASSIFICATION_HEADER` and re-reads the limits file on every request (no cache, so a change applies on the next request with no restart), and fails closed: anything other than the sharing-off or desktop-window classification, and a missing or unparseable classification, is a paired device subject to the limits; (3) that check called at the route, before any work, in `frontend/app/api/project/delete/route.ts` (Limit project deletion), in `frontend/app/api/encryption/route.ts` per action (`unlock` and `resume` under Limit unlocking; `lock` under Limit locking; `enable` on a project with the existing workspace key and `export` under Limit encryption changes; `enable` with a non-null passphrase, which creates the workspace keyring, always refused with no setting), and in the Trash purge route `frontend/app/api/project/[project-id]/trash/purge/route.ts` (Limit permanent deletion, for both a list of ids and `all: true`); the soft-delete routes (`frontend/app/api/resource/[resource-id]/delete/route.ts`, `frontend/app/api/folder/[folder-id]/delete/route.ts`) are untouched; (4) a refusal is a plain-message response that is not the gate's "not paired" response, so a refused device is not sent to the pairing screen; (5) one new read route that tells a caller whether it is a paired device and which of the five limits are on, gated like every route and added to the route enumeration tests (the walks over every `route.ts` that Features 75 and 76 keep); (6) conditional, built only if measurement (ii) shows a traversal value is accepted: the encryption route validates `projectId` for `enable` and `export` with the same fail-closed UUID guard the project-scoped routes use (`validateProjectId` and `respondInvalidProjectId` in `frontend/src/lib/models/project-path.ts`) before `enable-encryption.ts` joins it into a path, with a test that a traversal value is refused; if the value is rejected, the task is closed with a recorded "not needed". Interface: in the desktop app only, five switches in App Settings (`frontend/components/AppSettings/AppSettingsDialog.tsx`, in the sharing section `frontend/components/Sharing/SharingSettings.tsx`), each naming the limit with on meaning restricted (working labels: Limit project deletion, Limit unlocking, Limit locking, Limit encryption changes, Limit permanent deletion), the unlock switch carrying a plain warning beside it that switching the limit off means the passphrase is typed on the paired device and sent over the unencrypted connection, where others on the network could record it, and a recorded passphrase also unlocks the files at rest (a stolen disk or backup); `INTERIM_EXPOSURE_NOTE` in `frontend/components/Sharing/sharing-copy.ts` is replaced by text that reflects the actual state of the limits. The desktop window shows the limits in App Settings only. In the paired browser: (a) the safety requirement: while unlocking is limited, no passphrase prompt is offered, meaning the unlock dialog that `frontend/components/Start/StartPage.tsx` opens automatically for a locked workspace (`frontend/components/common/UnlockModal.tsx`) and the passphrase entry in `frontend/components/preferences/EncryptionSettings.tsx` and `frontend/components/common/EncryptionSetupModal.tsx`; the prompt is shown only after the read route has positively said the caller is exempt or the limit is off, so a failed or pending read shows no prompt; (b) controls for the limited actions shown disabled with the reason, not hidden (project deletion in `frontend/components/Start/ManageProjectMenu.tsx`, lock and the unlock entry, the project encryption panel in `frontend/components/preferences/ProjectEncryptionPanel.tsx`, and the Trash purge and empty-Trash controls in `frontend/components/WorkArea/Views/TrashView.tsx`); (c) the three behaviours read in the current client that must not survive, each stated as read, not run: a refused lock does nothing visible, a refused resume does nothing visible (`frontend/src/store/cryptoSlice.ts` has no rejected case for either), and project deletion removes the project from the list and shows "Project deleted" before a generic failure (`frontend/components/Start/ManageProjectMenu.tsx` ~65-81); the fix makes each refusal visible and puts the project list back to what the server holds. Never switchable, always refused: creating the workspace passphrase (no recovery path, ADR-022). Unchanged: the desktop window's abilities; a paired device deleting a resource or folder to the Trash; changing sharing, pairing codes and the device list, which stay desktop-only bridge calls with no network route.
specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2911:**Notes:** Rewritten 2026-10-10 twice. Gate 1 (Owner decision, Gate 1, 2026-10-10 (the pipeline lead's recommendation, accepted by the owner: 'Take your recs')): the earlier text (one opt-in setting for project deletion, and lock, unlock, sharing and pairing fixed as refused) is superseded in part by five limits, each a switch, all on by default. Gate 2 (Owner decision, Gate 2, 2026-10-10 (the pipeline lead's recommendation, accepted by the owner: 'Your recs')): the breakdown shown to the owner had four features (77, 81, 82, 83) and five numbered decisions, and the owner accepted them; this entry is the result, and Features 81, 82 and 83 were removed from the list (they were added earlier the same day and never committed). The five decisions: (1) the two measurements (formerly Feature 81) are folded into this feature as its first tasks, followed by a stop: results are reported to the owner before dependent work starts, the same shape Feature 75 used. (2) The paired-device experience (formerly Feature 83: limited controls shown disabled with the reason, and the three failure-visibility fixes) is folded in; nothing misleading ships separately. (3) The `projectId` validation fix for the encryption route (formerly Feature 82), if the measurement shows the flaw is real, is inside the release hold; the mechanism that gives effect to that with the least process is the pipeline lead's choice and is labelled as such: it is a conditional task inside this feature's task list, built only if measurement (ii) shows a traversal value is accepted and closed with a recorded "not needed" otherwise, so there is no separate feature. It remains a defect independent of sharing (OQ-85 in the product spec), and the feature spec and task list should say so. (4) The paired browser learns whether it is a paired device and which limits are on through one new read route, gated like every route and added to the route enumeration tests. (5) The desktop window shows the limits in App Settings only. Why one feature: server enforcement, the limits store with its desktop switches, the read route, the passphrase-prompt suppression and the visible-refusal behaviours are kept together because none is safe or honest to ship without the others. Enforcement without the store has nothing to read; the store without enforcement is a switch that does nothing; enforcement without the read route and the prompt suppression leaves a limited phone still offered the passphrase prompt on a locked workspace (the Start page opens it automatically; measured for a plain browser in `specs/features/home-network-sharing/experiments.md` section D), and a server refusal alone does not keep the passphrase off the unencrypted network, because the passphrase has been typed and sent by the time the server refuses; and enforcement without the disabled controls and visible refusals ships a default install (every limit on) in which a lock refusal is silent and a refused project deletion shows "Project deleted". Release hold: the hold on home-network sharing is on Features 77 and 80 being merged, as the owner set it. Because the paired-device experience and the conditional `projectId` task are inside this feature, they are inside the hold with no further condition added. No condition is added by the defaults: shipping with every limit on by default satisfies the hold as stated. Consequence of all five defaults being on: after upgrading, an already-paired device can no longer delete projects, lock, unlock, change encryption or purge until the writer switches that limit off at the desktop; Features 75 and 76 are merged and unreleased, so only the owner's own builds are affected. Hard ordering inside the feature: the two measurements come first and are followed by a stop; the limits hold only if measurement (i) shows the classification reaches a POST route handler in both the standalone build and `next dev`, with a forged client copy replaced; if it does not, the design here (enforcement at the route) changes and the owner is told before dependent work (OQ-85 in the product spec). A third observation is context only and is not in this feature's scope: on 2026-10-09 a `next dev` server under the desktop app was seen listening on `*:3000` with sharing off and answering a request to the host's LAN address with project data (`specs/features/paired-device-management/experiments.md`, "Exercise in the desktop app"); it is tracked separately and no fix is pulled in here. The test with two paired clients across an unlock and a lock, which Feature 75's Notes left to a later task list, belongs in this feature's task list. Because the unlock switch off sends the passphrase over plain HTTP, the warning copy is part of the feature, not polish; the other copy is for the single copy pass the owner deferred. Open points carried to the feature spec: where the limits file lives and how the server finds it. Nothing here was run. Status, 2026-10-10 (verified with git by the pipeline lead): MERGED to `main` and NOT RELEASED. PR #266, merge commit `fc730749`, branch `feat/paired-device-limits`, branch tip merged `8e157dd7`, merged 2026-10-10. With this merged, the release hold on home-network sharing waits on Feature 80 (port change) only; remaining home-network features 78, 79 and 80, all Not started. Feature spec `specs/features/paired-device-limits.md`, task list `specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md` (31 tasks, all ticked), measurements `specs/features/paired-device-limits/experiments.md`. Built: five limits (delete a project, unlock, lock, change encryption, permanently delete from the Trash), each a desktop switch on by default, global, effective on the next request; creating the workspace passphrase from a paired device is always refused; enforcement is at the route, answering 403 with a limit key; a missing or unparseable classification header is refused for every limited action. Owner decisions, 2026-10-10: the limits and their defaults; the encryption `projectId` fix shipped separately ahead of this feature as PR #265; a missing or unparseable classification header is refused unconditionally, whatever the limits file says (the feature spec's FR-10 wording was aligned); the old Feature 75 test allow-list of files importing the sharing library was updated to the four limited route files plus the limits read route; step 7 of the manual check (creating the passphrase from the phone) was accepted on scripted coverage; the copy is reviewed later in one pass. Verified, per the records: a built-server script against both the standalone build and `next dev` (195 checks, 0 failed, in each of the final standalone and `next dev` runs; every limited action covered); Playwright Chromium real-browser checks (107 checks, 0 failed); two paired clients across an unlock and a lock (31 checks, 0 failed); the real Electron app driven by script in development mode and in an unpacked build (167 checks each, 167 passed); and, reported by the owner, not observed by the pipeline, a manual check on the owner's Mac with an Android phone as the paired browser (steps 1 to 6, 8 and 9 "seem to work", no per-step detail; the unlock-off confirmation dialog appeared and was accepted as is). Suites: frontend 6434 passed, 1 skipped, reported by the implementing agent at the last change and not re-run by the lead; desktop 384; CLI 144; sharing smoke 91 enumerated entries, 28 checks, 0 failures; e2e 214 passed, 1 skipped, run locally by the lead after the Storybook fix. Defects found and fixed in the run: the pairing page reloaded itself for an unpaired device (found at the owner's manual check; 792 main-frame navigations in 8 s before the fix, 2 after, measured by the lead in Playwright; what the 2 are was not separated); a blank Start page when the limits read failed; revision-refusal wording that was inaccurate; dialog text with no colour class, black on the dark chrome background (measured: unclassed text under `<body>` is rgb(0, 0, 0) in both themes; which element the owner saw was not established); and Storybook had no limits provider, after which the e2e check on the PR failed. The failing e2e names were not seen, so the Storybook cause is inferred, and the lead did not see the CI result of the re-run before the merge (the owner reported afterwards, 2026-10-10, that CI passed). Not verified: Node 20 locally (none on the machine); creating the passphrase from the real phone (the owner's workspace already had one); the blank-Start-page, revision-wording and dialog-colour fixes re-measured in a browser; the rendered dark-mode dialogs; the sharing switch with restart in the scripted desktop runs. Known limits: an open page does not update on its own when the desktop locks or unlocks (7 s measured without an update); after a lock the paired browser's editor showed "Autosave failed", and the failing request was the save (PATCH of the revision, 404 `Revision ... not found`), cause not established, tracked separately. Deviations and incidents: the e2e suite was not part of the pipeline's final suites; no scripted check loaded the pairing page unpaired (their devices were paired by a helper or by a POST from Node), which is why none showed the reload; the passphrase gate sits in the Start page component, not in the page file. Found outside this feature, filed as notes and not fixed: soft-deleting a folder that contains folders returned HTTP 404 in the built-server script on both builds (not run on `main`, cause not established; the script's folder check therefore uses a leaf folder); `ConfirmDialog` details have no default text colour, so details text of other callers with no colour class would get the same black (callers not audited).

specs/product/getwrite.md:2806:**Addition, 2026-10-08 (Gate 3), copy and duplicate measured, and owner decision C:** Measured by the pipeline lead the same day, two ways. At model level (a temp project, the real functions, the scratch test deleted): `copyResourceCore` and `duplicateResource` each returned and persisted a sidecar that carried `resourceSubtype` and `entityKind` and the content directory, left `revisions/<newId>/` absent (0 revisions; the source had 1), and `loadProjectFromDisk` and `getLocalResources` both succeeded afterwards; `duplicateResource` also kept the source's `name` and `createdAt`. In the running app (`next dev`, a disposable projects directory, Playwright, files checked on disk): a baseline edit typed into a text resource reached `content.txt` and `content.tiptap.json` within 5 seconds (revisions: `v-1`); after Duplicate, an edit typed into the copy was in neither file after 8 and again about 25 seconds, no revision existed, and after a page reload and reopening the copy the edit was gone, with no toast, error or unsaved-changes prompt. Not measured: the menu's "Copy" action (Duplicate was used), image and audio copies, a manual "save revision" on the copy, and CLI `templates duplicate` output opened in the app. The cause is not established by experiment; triage's reading (the editor queues autosave only when editing the canonical revision, and a copy has none) is consistent with the measurement and untested. Owner decision C: fix it inside this work. A copy or duplicate of a text resource gets an initial canonical revision, written by the same code as a new resource's; the source's other revisions are not copied; image and audio copies get none. This is FR-64. Whether a duplicate should also get a fresh `createdAt` or a distinct name is not decided and is not part of this fix. Copies made before the fix are an open question in the feature spec, not decided here.
specs/product/getwrite.md:2910:**Resolution:** Enforcement is at the route, through one shared check that fails closed: anything other than the sharing-off or desktop-window classification is treated as a paired device subject to the limits, and a missing or unparseable classification is treated the same. Two experiments are prerequisites and this decision does not settle them. To be settled by experiment (plan task), result reported to the owner before dependent work: (i) that the gate's classification (the `x-getwrite-classification` header the gate sets) reaches a route handler on a POST with a JSON body, in the standalone build and in `next dev`, including that a forged client copy is replaced (measured so far only for a GET, Feature 75's experiments section E). To be settled by experiment (plan task), result reported to the owner before dependent work: (ii) whether the encryption route validates the `projectId` it joins into a path for `enable` and `export`; triage found no validation by reading (`frontend/app/api/encryption/route.ts` ~195-204, `enable-encryption.ts` ~118); if a traversal value is accepted, that is a defect to fix regardless of this feature.

specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:38:**Done when:** `experiments.md` records the exact commands (for example `rg -n -w '3000'` with the excluded directories named, plus searches for `localhost:3000`, `127.0.0.1:3000`, `:3000`, `PORT`, `-p 3000`, `--port`, `process.env.PORT`, `next dev`, `next start`, and ports written without the literal such as `3e3` or a port computed from another value) and their FULL output, covering source, tests, docs, workflows, scripts and `specs/`. Every hit is classified as (a) follows the single source, (b) hard-coded elsewhere, (c) implicit default with no literal, (d) historical record, or (e) unrelated (a duration, word count, test count), and ends with a disposition (follows the source, changed, deliberately left, or historical) and a reason. The starting list of FR-6 is checked against the result and every difference is listed, including any hit the list missed. The expected dispositions per the Gate 3 answers are recorded as owner decisions: hosted-auth fallbacks and their two tests left; the `paired-device-limits` scripts left (historical, not valid against the new port, and why); the AppImage smoke workflow left except for a comment (Task 11). Every "changed" disposition names the test or check that will cover it (FR-24). `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
~~~~

#### Appendix 8. Command 10, listen / createServer / port assignment (324)

docs/features/feature-specifications/markdown-support/markdown-support-spec.md:53:- **Editing model:** source/rich toggle. **Lossy export:** HTML fallback + warning.
docs/features/revisions.md:70:### `setRevisionPreserve(projectRoot, resourceId, revisionId, preserve)`
docs/features/revisions.md:81:- The `RevisionTransport` method is `setPreserve`; the exported wrapper in `revision-transport-service.ts` is `persistRevisionPreserve(context, revisionId, preserve)`. The native backend calls `setRevisionPreserve` directly.
docs/features/word-count-goals.md:13:- Client transport: `frontend/src/lib/api/word-count-goal.ts` — `setWordCountGoal(projectId, goal)`, resolved through `createTransport`: HTTP on web/desktop (`httpWordCountGoalTransport`), an in-process backend (`frontend/src/store/transport/native-word-count-goal-backend.ts`, + `.web-stub.ts`) on native, both running the same core. The HTTP path validates its response body against `SetWordCountGoalResponseSchema` (`lib/api/schemas.ts`) and rejects (reporting through `reportTransportValidationFailure`) on a malformed body, network error, or non-2xx status — the save has by then already succeeded server-side, so the caller re-reads rather than trusting a fabricated value.
docs/features/word-count-goals.md:22:- Transport: **no new transport module was needed.** This is the Task 3 finding this doc is here to record: the resource goal rides the _existing_ sidecar transport, `updateSidecar` (`frontend/src/lib/api/resources.ts`), because that transport's HTTP path never validated its response body for any field to begin with — it is fire-and-forget by design. Clearing goes through `updateSidecar`'s `clearKeys` parameter (`["wordCountGoal"]`), not an `undefined`-valued field in the body, since `JSON.stringify` drops an `undefined`-valued key before the request ever reaches the server (the same bug class Feature 61's own brief on `patchRevisionContent` names).
docs/refactor/deltas/01-foundation.md:33:- Named the default export: `const lockApi = { acquireLock }; export default lockApi`. Fixes
docs/refactor/deltas/06-projects.md:22:- **consistency** — Named the anonymous default export: `const projectCreator = { createProjectFromType }; export default projectCreator`. Resolves the pre-existing `import/no-anonymous-default-export` lint warning and aligns with the pattern used in `tags.ts`.
docs/refactor/deltas/10-shell.md:158:- `TimelineView` / `TimelineViewProps` duplication between `TimelineView.tsx` and its `index.ts` re-export: pre-existing knip duplicate-export finding, not introduced by this slice.

specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md:125:**What:** Call the shared check in `postEncryptionAction` per action (unlock and resume: unlocking; lock: locking; enable with a non-null passphrase: always refused; enable with a null passphrase and export: encryption changes) before any session, storage or conversion call.
specs/features/paired-device-limits/tasks.md:147:**Done when:** tests first, seen failing, then green. The route answers `{ "role": "exempt" }` for classification `off` and `window` and does not read the limits file for them (spy); `{ "role": "paired", "limits": { five booleans } }` for `confirmed` with a fresh read each call; `paired` with all five true for a `confirmed` caller when the file is missing or unreadable (a positive answer, not an error), and for a missing or unparseable classification header; `Cache-Control` contains `no-store`; the file changed between two requests changes the answer. The route is reached only after the gate (an unpaired caller is refused by the gate, asserted in the proxy-level enumeration), is not in `ENUMERATED_EXCEPTIONS` (which stays `GET /pair` and `POST /api/sharing/pair`, exception count 3), appears in both `sharing-gate-enumeration.test.ts` and `sharing-refusal-smoke.mjs` enumerations (the smoke script gets the entry or discovers it by walking; state which), and the route file and the shared check do not contain the strings `devices` nor import the credential store (`sharing-no-device-routes.test.ts` passes unchanged). The enumeration entry count and the smoke check count are measured and recorded in the report: before (Task 26 will confirm the 90 / 28 reference) and after. From `frontend/`: the new test, the two sharing enumeration tests, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`; `pnpm knip` nothing new. `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` is NOT run here (needs a build); Task 26 runs it.
specs/features/protect-revision/tasks.md:15:**What:** Add `setRevisionPreserve(projectRoot, resourceId, revisionId, preserve: boolean)` to `frontend/src/lib/models/revision-core.ts`, which merges `preserve` into the revision's existing `metadata` (leaving `name` and other keys unchanged), works on canonical and non-canonical revisions, throws `Revision ${revisionId} not found.` for an unknown id, and returns the updated `Revision`.
specs/features/protect-revision/tasks.md:42:**What:** Add a `setPreserve(context, revisionId, preserve)` method to the `RevisionTransport` interface and `httpRevisionTransport` in `frontend/src/store/revision-transport-service.ts` (hitting Task 4's PATCH mode) and to `frontend/src/store/transport/native-revision-backend.ts` (in-process call to Task 2's function via the same runner pattern as `setCanonical`/`delete`), plus the matching throw-if-reached method in `native-revision-backend.web-stub.ts`, with a public wrapper resolving through `createTransport`.
