specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:61:- The desktop app's bundled server listens on 7340, and the window loads from `http://localhost:7340`, with sharing off and with sharing on.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:74:- Editing recorded measurements in older specs and experiments files to say 7340.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:78:- US-1: As a writer who shares my projects on my home network, I want to have the desktop app serve on port 7340 so that the address I give my other devices is not a generic default shared with other local software.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:88:FR-1: The constant `PORT` in `electron/src/server-config.ts` (read, not run: line 11, `export const PORT = 3000;`) MUST become 7340. No other source file in `electron/src/` MUST gain a numeric port literal. The port is a fixed constant (OQ-7, resolved): no environment override and no writer-facing setting MUST be added. [US-1]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:90:FR-2: The existing guard tests MUST still pass unchanged: `electron/tests/server-config.test.ts` (exactly one numeric export, `PORT`; `main.ts` assigns no numeric literal to a port; read, not run). One test pinning the value 7340 MAY be added in that file only, because Feature 75 FR-25 forbids any other test depending on the value. [US-1]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:92:FR-3: Each consumer of the desktop port MUST keep reading it from the single source, and a unit test MUST show it with a port other than 3000 or 7340 passed in as input. The consumers, read, not run: the server's `PORT` env (`electron/src/main.ts` line 173); the window load, retry and readiness URLs (lines 732, 738, 745); the navigation guard (line 683); the IPC sender checks (lines 292, 300, 304); the window-secret installation (line 782); the sharing status port (line 313) and `candidateAddresses(interfaces, port)` in `electron/src/sharing/addresses.ts`. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:94:FR-4: The server's own port MUST continue to come from its `PORT` env, which the main process sets, and not from a constant or the request. (Read, not run: `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts` line 158 calls `parseServerPort(env.PORT)` and refuses every request when it is missing or not a plain decimal in 1..65535; `host-allowlist.ts` builds accepted names as `<name>:<port>`; `same-origin.ts` compares `Origin`/`Referer` to `Host` and has no port constant.) A test MUST show a Host of `<lan-ip>:<PORT>` accepted and the same name on another port refused, with `PORT=7340`. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:100:FR-7: The linux AppImage smoke workflow MUST NOT be pointed at 7340 on the strength of this feature alone (OQ-9, resolved). Read, not run: it downloads the published releases 1.8.0 and 2.0.0 (`gh release download getwrite-v${{ matrix.version }}`) and probes `http://localhost:3000`; those builds were released with 3000 and the workflow tests them. The only workflow change MUST be a comment near its "nothing may answer on :3000" check (`.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml`, around line 138) saying that a future matrix entry for a build on 7340 needs its own probe port; no probe, matrix or step is changed. The disposition is recorded under FR-24. [US-7]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:102:FR-8: The scripts under `specs/features/paired-device-limits/scripts/` are historical records. Read, not run: they refuse to run when port 3000 has a listener and `electron-limits.mjs` line 81 sets `BASE` to `http://127.0.0.1:3000`, so against a build with 7340 they would test the wrong address. They MUST NOT be edited by this feature (OQ-8, resolved); the new experiments file MUST say they are not valid against the new port and why. [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:120:FR-17: With sharing on, the Host allowlist MUST accept `<lan-ip>:7340`, `localhost:7340` and `127.0.0.1:7340` and MUST refuse `<lan-ip>:3000` and `localhost:3000`, shown against a built server started on a free port set through `PORT` and, separately, against the real app. A request sent from the host to its own LAN address is not a second-device result and MUST be labelled so. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:122:FR-18: With sharing on, every address shown in the desktop window (the status and settings surfaces) MUST end in `:7340`, and none MUST end in `:3000`, shown in the real Electron app in development mode and in an unpacked build, each with a throwaway `--user-data-dir` and a throwaway projects folder. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:124:FR-19: In the same two real-app runs, with sharing off and with sharing on, the window MUST load its content from `http://localhost:7340`, and a request to `http://localhost:3000` MUST NOT reach the app's server (checked only after confirming nothing else listens on 3000). [US-1]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:126:FR-20: Pairing MUST be exercised end to end on the new port by loading `/pair` UNPAIRED in a real browser (Chromium through Playwright, a fresh browser context with no cookie) at `http://<lan-ip>:7340`, typing a pairing code obtained from the real app's own window flow, and reaching the paired application. This is required because Feature 77's pairing-page defect was found only that way and its scripted runs, which paired by a helper, did not show it; the run MUST also count main-frame navigations on the pairing page. [US-2]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:130:FR-22: Port in use MUST be measured twice, and each record MUST say which port it used and why. First, before the constant changes, today's mechanism on 3000 with a throwaway listener (valid for the mechanism, because the readiness and fork code is the same for any port; it says nothing about 7340 itself). Then again on the changed build on 7340. For the 7340 run: the task starts its own throwaway listener on 7340 (after confirming nothing else listens there), launches the real app in development mode and in an unpacked build against throwaway folders, and records what the window shows, whether the window loaded the listener's response, whether the listener received the window header (recorded as present or absent only, never its value), the app's exit and log lines, and what the sharing status reports. The listener MUST be stopped by its own recorded process id. Results go to the owner before FR-11's behaviour is built. [US-4]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:138:FR-26: Owner decision (OQ-6 = (b), Gate 3, 2026-10-10): the block-of-ten convention MUST be recorded as a new ADR in `docs/architecture/ADRs/` (`adr-023-...`, the next number after adr-022, following the existing ADR format) plus a line in `CLAUDE.md`. Each MUST state GetWrite's block (7340 to 7349), that the next Saboteur app starts at base plus 10 (7350), and that the Gate 0 screening was against a local `/etc/services` and not the live registry. A copy in a shared cross-repo location is the owner's own step, outside this feature and this repository, and is not made by the pipeline. [US-5]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:140:FR-27: Process safety. Any task that starts a server or app MUST: check the app's port (7340, and 3000 when a development server may be involved) for a listener first and refuse to run if one exists; start servers on free ports it picks itself where the check does not need 7340; stop only the process ids it started and recorded, never by port and never a listener's parent; use throwaway user-data and projects folders; never read or write `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/`; and never print or record credentials, cookies, secrets or pairing codes. (On 2026-10-10 a pipeline script stopped every listener on port 3000, including the owner's running desktop app; PDM experiments, "A run that does not count, and a mistake".) [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:144:FR-29: The final suites MUST be run against the FR-9 baselines and reported with counts: frontend `test:ci`, electron, CLI, typecheck for frontend and electron, lint, knip, `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` against a built standalone server (read, not run: `frontend/scripts/sharing-refusal-smoke.mjs` picks a free port itself and sets `PORT`, so it should not depend on 7340; to be shown), and `pnpm test:e2e` against Storybook on 6006. Any difference from baseline MUST be listed with its cause as measured or as unestablished. [US-6]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:146:FR-30: The owner's manual check with the Android phone MUST be listed as a task the implementer cannot do: with sharing on, open `http://<lan-ip>:7340` on the phone, record what a phone paired before the change shows (this is FR-14's real-device counterpart), pair again if asked, and confirm an edit made on the phone appears on the desktop. It is reported by the owner and is not an observation of the pipeline. [US-3]
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:156:- OQ-2 (provisional, open until the FR-23 stop): Does development mode move to 7340, stay on 3000, or take its own port in the block, and should a packaged app and a dev app be able to run at once? Evidence: read, not run, `electron:dev` already passes `PORT` to `next dev`, so it follows the constant; plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` do not and default to 3000; the demo scripts and `demo/README.md` assume 3000; the QA harness picks a free port and is unaffected; whether the packaged and dev apps share a single-instance lock was not established. Options: (a) `electron:dev` follows the constant, `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000; (b) all of them move to 7340 (`next dev -p`), so only one runs at a time; (c) dev takes a second port in the block (7341) so both run at once, with the demo scripts following; (d) all stay on 3000 and `electron:dev` is given its own override. Trade-off: (a) leaves the 3000 collision the owner wants to avoid in the everyday dev command; (b) removes it and forbids running both; (c) allows both and spends a number in the block. Provisional direction (accepted by the owner for writing the task list, to be brought back at the FR-23 stop): (a), with (c) an optional task the stop can turn on depending on whether a dev app and a packaged app can run at once (single-instance lock keying is unverified). — Impact: FR-12, FR-16, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:176:- OQ-1 (resolved): What should happen when the port is already in use at launch? Resolution: option (a). The app refuses to start the server and shows a plain message naming the port; there is no fallback port. Independently, the readiness check must accept only the server the app itself forked. Evidence (read, not run): `electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` lines 49 to 53 resolve on any status below 500 and send the window secret header on every poll; `main.ts` raises "Server exited unexpectedly" only on a non-zero exit. Hypothesis, not yet measured: a foreign listener answering below 500 passes readiness and receives the header (FR-22 measures it, first on 3000 with a throwaway listener to establish the mechanism, then again on 7340 after the constant changes). The window secret must never be sent to a process the app did not start. The mechanism (for example a per-launch nonce the child echoes, or the child's exit or bind state) is a design choice for the task; the size of that work is to be confirmed by the FR-22 measurement. Impact: FR-10, FR-11, FR-22.
specs/features/desktop-server-port.md:181:- OQ-9 (resolved): Does the AppImage smoke workflow change? Resolution: it keeps probing 3000 because it tests the published 1.8.0 and 2.0.0 releases; a comment near its "nothing may answer on :3000" check says a future matrix entry for a 7340 build needs its own probe port. No other workflow change. Impact: FR-7, FR-24.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:13:- **Process-safety rules, repeated by every task that starts a server or app (FR-27).** Before starting anything: check for a listener on the port the app will use (3000 before Task 8 lands, 7340 after) and on every port the task itself uses (`lsof -nP -iTCP:<port> -sTCP:LISTEN`), and REFUSE to run if the app's port is busy, unless the task itself created that throwaway listener (Tasks 3 and 6 and the port-in-use steps of 17 and 19). Servers the task starts use a free port the task picks itself unless the task is checking 7340 or 3000 on purpose. Record the pids the task starts and stop only those pids, never by port, never a listener's parent or a process the task did not start (on 2026-10-10 a pipeline script stopped every listener on port 3000, including the owner's running desktop app). Use throwaway user-data and projects folders under `$TMPDIR`, assert the paths in the script before using them, and NEVER read or write `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/`. Never print or record credentials, cookies, secrets, window-secret values or pairing codes; header presence is recorded as present or absent only. Scripts live under `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/` (not under `frontend/`, so `pnpm knip` does not see them) and are described in `experiments.md`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:17:- **Port statement.** Measurements before Task 8 can only be made on 3000, the port the code uses until the constant changes; measuring the port-in-use mechanism on 3000 with a throwaway listener is valid for the mechanism and says nothing about 7340. After Task 8 the same measurement is repeated on 7340 (Tasks 17 and 19).
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:28:**Done when:** the section records, with command, date, exit status, counts, `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `node --version`: from `frontend/`, `pnpm test:ci` (passed, failed, skipped), `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` (errors, warnings), `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` (checks and failures; after `pnpm build` and copying `.next/static` into `.next/standalone/frontend/.next/static`), and `pnpm test:e2e` (passed, failed, skipped, against `pnpm storybook` on 6006, with the Storybook pid started by the task and stopped by that pid); from `electron/`, `pnpm test` and `pnpm typecheck`; from the repo root, `pnpm knip` with the issue count per section AND the list of names (so later runs are compared by set), and `pnpm --filter getwrite-cli test`. The record also states the reported reference figures (Feature 77: frontend 6434 passed and 1 skipped, desktop 384, CLI 144, smoke 28 checks, e2e 214 passed and 1 skipped) and marks them "reported, not re-run" unless the run reproduces them. A failing baseline suite is recorded as failing with its test names and not fixed. Before starting, the task checks 3000, 6006 and 7340 for listeners and refuses if 6006 is busy; any server it starts uses a free port it picks, and it stops only the pids it started. `git status --short` afterwards shows no change outside `specs/`, apart from the spec work already present at the start (paste the output).
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:48:**Done when:** `experiments.md` states in its first lines WHICH PORT this used and why: 3000, because the constant has not changed yet; valid for the mechanism; says nothing about 7340; Task 17 and Task 19 repeat it on 7340. It records, using the dev-mode copied-tree approach and a throwaway `--user-data-dir` and projects folder: that 3000 had no listener before the task's own throwaway listener started (the task refuses to run otherwise); the throwaway listener is a tiny HTTP server on 3000 answering a distinct marker page with status 200, started by the task, its pid recorded, which records only the PRESENCE (yes/no) of the `x-getwrite-window` request header on each request it receives and never the value; for the app launched against it: what the window showed (screenshot description or window title and a DOM text excerpt that contains no secret), whether the window loaded the listener's page (yes/no), whether the listener received the window header (present or absent only, and how many requests), whether the app showed "Server exited unexpectedly" or any other message, the forked server child's exit code and the app log lines (names and messages only), what the sharing status reported, and whether the app exited. A second variant with a listener answering status 500 and a third with no response at all (accepts the connection and stays silent) are recorded the same way if they run without extra setup. The reading in FR-10 ("resolves on any status below 500, sends the secret on every poll") is stated as read, and the run is stated as the measurement; a cause is named only where a variant discriminates it. The record ends with a plain statement for Tasks 9 and 10 of which of these hold today: the window showed a foreign page; the header reached a foreign process; a failed bind of the child is or is not visible to the app. The app and listener are stopped by their recorded pids and the throwaway folders removed. `git status --short` shows no change outside `specs/`.
specs/features/desktop-server-port/tasks.md:71:**Notes:** Satisfies FR-14 and the evidence for OQ-4. Main checkout, outside the sandbox, after `pnpm build` and copying `.next/static` into `.next/standalone/frontend/.next/static` (the build needs 3000 and 7340 free only to start nothing; the servers use free ports A and B). A request from the host to its own LAN address is not a second-device result: label it so. If the machine has no LAN address, record that and stop.

specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2938:**Notes:** Raised by the owner at Gate 2, 2026-10-09: "I've been wanting to change it from 3000 (due to its default nature), that step may need to precede this work, and I don't know what address I want to use, but other Saboteur apps will be offset from it". Not decided, open points inside this feature: the port value itself (the owner has not chosen it; other Saboteur apps' ports will be offset from whatever GetWrite's is), and whether it is a fixed constant or configurable. Ordering (owner decision, 2026-10-09: "Port change proposal is fine, 80 before release"): Feature 75 is specified against a single port source and does not depend on its value, so Feature 80 need not precede 75's build; but Feature 80 MUST be merged before any release containing Feature 75, because a paired device saves the desktop's address (host and port), so changing the port after writers have paired devices would break their saved addresses. Together with the Gate 2 decision, the release containing Feature 75 is held until Features 76, 77 and 80 are all merged (76 has since merged, 2026-10-10). Nothing here was run. (Update, 2026-10-10: Features 76 and 77 have since merged, so the release hold waits on this feature only.) Owner decision, Gate 0, 2026-10-10: the desktop app's bundled server port becomes 7340 (was 3000), and Saboteur apps take blocks of ten: GetWrite owns 7340 to 7349, the next Saboteur app starts at base plus 10 (7350), and so on. This settles the port value above; whether the port is a fixed constant or configurable is still open. Screening done by the pipeline lead, stated as facts and not as guarantees: on the owner's Mac, `/etc/services` (a local copy of the registry, not the live IANA list) registers no TCP service in 7340 to 7349, 7350 to 7359, 7370 to 7379 or 7380 to 7389, while 7365 is registered in the 7360 block and 7391 to 7395 and 7397 in the 7390 block; 7340 is not among the common development defaults (3000, 4200, 5000, 5173, 6006, 7000, 8000, 8080, 8888, 9000) and lies between 1024 and 32767. That 7340 is absent from Chrome's and Firefox's restricted-port lists is from memory and was not checked against the browsers' current lists; the feature spec requires loading the app in real browsers to verify it. Feature spec: `specs/features/desktop-server-port.md`. The final suites for this feature include `pnpm test:e2e` alongside the unit suites, typecheck, lint, knip, the CLI suite and `pnpm test:sharing-smoke` (owner decision, Gate 0, 2026-10-10). Standing decisions carried from Features 75 to 77: working-copy strings are reviewed in one later copy pass; the Android app as a client is later.
~~~~

#### Appendix 10. Command 12, workflows, any port or localhost (3)

~~~~text
.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml:138:                    # forked, so nothing may answer on :3000. Checking only the
.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml:67:                    if [ -d "$HOME/Documents/GetWrite" ] || curl -sf -o /dev/null http://localhost:3000; then
.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml:94:                  if curl -sf -o /dev/null http://localhost:3000; then
~~~~

### Classification and disposition of every hit

Classes: (a) follows the single source; (b) hard-coded elsewhere; (c) implicit default with no literal; (d) historical record; (e) unrelated (a duration, a word count, a test count, a list name). Dispositions: follows the source, changed, deliberately left, historical. "Provisional" means the disposition rests on OQ-2, which the owner accepted only provisionally and which is finalized at Task 7.

How the table was made, stated plainly: the 337 hits of command 1 were assigned by a rule script (`classify.py`, scratchpad; first match wins on file path, with a text test only for the duration/timeout hits and for two line-specific cases). The script exits with an error if any hit matches no rule; it did not. I read the text of every non-`specs/` hit in a listing cut at 200 to 260 columns before writing the rules; the `specs/` hits were read in a listing cut at 210 columns and assigned by file, not line by line. The table groups the lines of one file that have the same class, disposition and reason; the number in brackets is how many hits that row covers; the per-line text is in Appendix 1.

| Location | Class | Disposition | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml`: 67, 94 (2) | b | left | W1 |
| `.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml`: 138 (1) | b | left (comment added by Task 11) | W2 |
| `CLAUDE.md`: 219 (1) | b | changed (documentation stage) | D1 |
| `README.md`: 106 (1) | b | left | H2 |
| `cli/src/qa/server.ts`: 15, 469 (2) | e | left | Q1 |
| `cli/tests/qa/server.test.ts`: 234, 238, 243, 244, 249, 296, 300 (7) | b | left | Q2 |
| `cli/tests/qa/server.test.ts`: 451 (1) | b | left | Q3 |

### Class (a) without the literal (found by searching for `PORT`)

These hits contain no `3000`; they read the port from the single source or the `PORT` env. Read, not run. Disposition for each: follows the source.

| Location | What it does | Covering test or check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `electron/src/main.ts:15` | imports `PORT, localOrigin` from `./server-config` | Task 8 consumer tests |
| `electron/src/main.ts:173` | server env `PORT: String(PORT)` | Task 8 (FR-3), Tasks 17 and 19 (listening port by pid) |
| `electron/src/main.ts:292, 300, 304` | IPC sender checks `isTrustedSender(url, localOrigin(PORT))` | Task 8 (FR-3) |
| `electron/src/main.ts:313` | sharing status `port: PORT` | Task 8 (FR-3), Tasks 17 and 19 (addresses shown) |
| `electron/src/main.ts:683` | navigation guard `isLocalOriginUrl(url, localOrigin(PORT))` | Task 8 (FR-3) |
| `electron/src/main.ts:732, 738, 745` | window load, retry load, readiness url `localOrigin(PORT)` | Task 8 (FR-3), Task 9 |
| `electron/src/main.ts:782` | window-secret installation `localOrigin(PORT)` | Task 8 (FR-3), Task 9 |
| `electron/src/sharing/addresses.ts:62` | `candidateAddresses(interfaces, port)` takes the port as an argument | Task 8 (a port other than 3000 or 7340 as input; the existing `addresses.test.ts` passes 3000 as input) |
| `frontend/src/lib/sharing/gate.ts:158`, `frontend/src/lib/sharing/host-allowlist.ts:70-76`, `frontend/app/api/sharing/pair/route.ts:75` | the server's own port from `process.env.PORT` through `parseServerPort` (a plain decimal in 1..65535, else refuse everything) | Task 8 (FR-4 Host test with `PORT=7340`), Task 15 (built server) |
| `electron/tests/device-ipc.test.ts:11,18,28,30` and `electron/tests/limits-ipc.test.ts:12,14,23,25` | build the trusted origin from `PORT` and two untrusted senders, `http://localhost:${PORT}@evil.example/` and `http://localhost:${PORT}1/` ("lookalike port") | existing tests; **see the finding below** |

Finding for Task 8 (a code fact, read, plus the one measured `URL` check above): with `PORT = 7340` the "lookalike port" sender in those two test files becomes `http://localhost:73401/`. Node v24.15.0 rejects that URL (`ERR_INVALID_URL`, port above 65535). `isTrustedSender` catches the parse error (`navigation-guard.ts`, `parsedOrigin` returns `null`) and returns false, so each test would still pass, but it would pass for the parse error and no longer for the origin comparison it names. The tests were not run. Which fix Task 8 chooses (a different lookalike, e.g. appending a character that keeps the number valid) is Task 8's decision; this task changes no test.

Not (c): `frontend/package.json:16` `storybook dev -p 6006`, `frontend/scripts/serve-storybook-static.mjs:33` (`process.env.PORT ?? 6006`), `frontend/playwright.config.ts:42` (`port: 6006`) and `.mcp.json:5` (6006) name Storybook's port; `frontend/scripts/sharing-refusal-smoke.mjs:125-127,222,228` and `cli/src/qa/server.ts:153-156,497,516` pick a free port with `listen(0, ...)` and pass it as `PORT`; `scripts/hosted-smoke/compose.yml` uses host ports 55432, 51025 and 58025 (header comment: "deliberately non-default"). ### Ports written without the literal, or computed

Command 9 (`3e3`, `3_000`, `3*1000`, `30*100`, `0xBB8`, `"3000"`, `'3000'`) found 6 lines: three `vi.stubEnv("PORT", "3000")` calls (`sharing-gate-enumeration.test.ts:166`, `sharing-gate-proxy.test.ts:58`, `sharing-pair-route.test.ts:87`; F1, they feed the `PORT` env as a fixture), two `wordCountGoalSection.test.tsx` lines that type "3000" into a word-count field (E2), and one line of `desktop-server-port/tasks.md` that is the task text itself (S0). No `3e3`, `3_000`, product or hex form was found. Command 10 (any `listen`, `createServer`, `port:` or `port =` assignment) returned 324 lines (Appendix 8); reading the non-test source among them (`electron/src`, `cli/src`, `frontend/scripts`, `frontend/app`, `frontend/src/lib/sharing`, `frontend/src/lib/auth`, `scripts/`, `demo/`, `android/`) found no port derived from another value (no `base + offset`); the only port-valued computations are `Number(process.env.PORT ?? 6006)` (Storybook server), `parseServerPort` (parse of the `PORT` env), `Number(portRaw)` in `frontend/src/lib/auth/email.ts:104` (SMTP) and the free-port probes. This is the result of those patterns on tracked files; it is not a proof that no other form exists.

Command 11 (`-w 7340`) found 66 lines: all in `specs/features/desktop-server-port.md` and `.../tasks.md`, and one in `specs/product/getwrite.features.md:2938`. No source, test, doc or workflow file names 7340 yet.

Command 12 (every workflow, any port): `.github/workflows/linux-appimage-smoke.yml:67,94,138` only. The other seven workflows (`build-android`, `build-electron`, `cli-smoke-tests`, `e2e-tests`, `electron-checks`, `frontend-checks`, `release-please`) name no port and no `localhost`.

### Difference from the FR-6 starting list

Every item the list names was found at the stated line (`auth-server.ts:273`, `better-auth.config.ts:86`, `with-storage-context.ts:155,165`, `auth-server.test.ts:265`, `better-auth-cli-config.test.ts:61`, `README.md:106`, `scripts/hosted-smoke/smoke.mjs:36` and its README, `openapi.yaml:9,25`, `demo/seed.mjs`, `demo/playwright.demo.config.ts`, `demo/README.md`, `CLAUDE.md:219`, `docs/features/home-network-sharing.md:73,173`, the workflow lines 67, 94 and 138, `cli/src/qa/server.ts`, `cli/tests/qa/server.test.ts`, the `paired-device-limits` scripts, and the named fixture tests). The differences:

1. **`better-auth-cli-config.test.ts:61` is not an assertion of the fallback.** It sets `BETTER_AUTH_URL` to `http://localhost:3000` as an input (H1T). Only `auth-server.test.ts:265` asserts the fallback. Disposition is the same (left).
2. **`electron/src/navigation-guard.ts:4-5`** (comment, C1) is not on the list.
3. **`electron/tests/server-config.test.ts:11`** (3000 among the `it.each` inputs) is not on the list (F1, passes unchanged).
4. **`localhost:30001` look-alike strings** are not matched by `-w 3000` and are on no list: `navigation-guard.ts:5`, `electron/tests/window-secret.test.ts:108`, `specs/features/paired-device-management.md:123`. They are a different port written as a longer number; F1/C1/S1.
5. **`frontend/tests/unit/sharing-classify-request.test.ts:103` and `sharing-limits-route.test.ts:54`** are 3000 fixtures not named by the list (which names the gate, same-origin, host-allowlist and pair-route tests). F1, unchanged. The two gate files are `sharing-gate-enumeration.test.ts` and `sharing-gate-proxy.test.ts`.
6. **37 class (e) hits** (durations, word counts, list names) are on no list; none is a port. They would have been false positives for a reader of the starting list.
7. **`electron/tests/device-ipc.test.ts` and `limits-ipc.test.ts` derive an origin from `PORT`** and carry the `${PORT}1` lookalike that becomes an invalid URL at 7340 (see the finding above). Not on the list because they contain no literal.
8. **Three server-side readers of the `PORT` env** (`gate.ts:158`, `host-allowlist.ts:70-76`, `pair/route.ts:75`) are in FR-4 but not in FR-6; class (a).
9. **The class (c) lines** are not enumerated in the starting list, which names `next dev` and `next start` in prose only: the seven lines in the class (c) table, including `demo/playwright.demo.config.ts:24` and `scripts/hosted-smoke/README.md:72`.
10. **Coverage of "changed" dispositions (FR-24).** Of the two "changed" dispositions, only the constant (K1) has tests in the task list. The `CLAUDE.md:219` change (D1) is a documentation edit at the FR-25 stage, which `tasks.md` states is outside the list, so no task owns a check for it. A re-run of command 1 restricted to `CLAUDE.md` after the documentation stage would be that check; nothing schedules it. This is a gap in the task list, not a decision.
11. **`specs/` hits other than the `paired-device-limits` scripts** (152 hits in 15 files: 62 in this feature's own two files, class (d) row S0, and 90 in 13 other files, class (d) row S1) are not on the starting list, which names only those scripts; FR-5 required `specs/` to be searched and it was.

## Port in use today (3000)

**Which port: 3000.** The constant in `electron/src/server-config.ts` has not changed yet, so the unchanged app can only be measured on 3000. The result is valid for the mechanism (the fork, readiness and exit-handling code does not depend on the number) and says nothing about 7340. Tasks 17 and 19 repeat the measurement on 7340.

Task 3. Date 2026-10-10. Main checkout, branch `feat/desktop-server-port`, tree clean before (the only change afterwards is this file and `scripts/`). Node v24.15.0. Script: `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/port-in-use-today.mjs`, run as `node port-in-use-today.mjs <ok|500|silent> [listener-host]`. `electron/dist` was rebuilt first (`pnpm build && pnpm build:worker` in `electron/`, because `electron/src/main.ts` had a newer file time than `dist/main.js`; the source was unedited).

### Method

- **Outside the command sandbox** (`dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true`) for every run: Electron, Playwright `_electron`, the listener and the dev server need it.
- **Pre-check:** `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` printed nothing before every run (and the script refuses to run otherwise, then requires that the only listener after its own listener starts is the listener's pid). 6006 and 7340 were not used and not checked.
- **Listener:** this same script started as a separate process (`--listener`), so it has its own pid. It answers `GET /` and prints one JSON line per request: time, method, path, a client kind (`node-http` when the request has no `User-Agent`, `chromium-like` otherwise) and whether the `x-getwrite-window` header was present (yes/no). It never reads or prints the header value. Variants: `ok` (200 with a page whose title and text are `FOREIGN-LISTENER-MARKER`), `500` (status 500, text body), `silent` (parses the request and never answers; the connection stays open).
- **App:** the real Electron app in "the development code path in a copied tree": a throwaway fake repo root with a copy of `electron/dist` and `electron/package.json`, symlinks to the real `frontend/` and `getwrite-config/`, an empty `projects/`, Electron started on `<fake>/electron` with a throwaway `--user-data-dir`. Dev mode therefore forks `pnpm dev` (`next dev`) in the real `frontend/`; the difference from `electron dist/main.js` in the repo tree was not measured, and the **packaged build was not run** (it forks the standalone server through `utilityProcess`, a different child kind). Sharing was left at its default (off); it was not turned on for any run.
- **Deviation from Feature 77's copied-tree approach:** the copied `package.json` was given a throwaway `name` and no `productName`, so the app's own log (`app.getPath("logs")`, which `--user-data-dir` does not move) went to a throwaway folder under `~/Library/Logs` instead of appending to the owner's log; the script asserts `app.getPath("logs")` is that folder and removes it. The app's log file there is what the "log lines" below are.
- **Asserted before anything was touched:** the throwaway paths are under `os.tmpdir()` (resolved outside the sandbox) and not inside the owner's userData; after launch `app.getPath("userData")` equals the throwaway folder and `isPackaged` is `false`. `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` was not read, listed or written. No cookie, pairing code, window-secret value or response body was printed or recorded.
- **Sampling:** once a second for 55 s, or until the app process exits, the script reads the first window's URL, title and `document.body.innerText` (first 400 characters) from the main process, and reads the sharing status through the window's own bridge (`window.getwriteDesktop.getSharingStatus()`), keeping only `enabled`, `effective`, `blockedByHostedAuth`, `port`, the number of addresses and `credentialStore`. Timestamps `t=` are milliseconds since the script's start. Window changes are seen at one-second granularity, so a `t=` for a window change is an upper bound.
- **Four runs** (one app per run; each run's output was kept and is summarised here, not pasted whole). An earlier set of three runs (`ok` on `127.0.0.1`, `ok` on `::`, `500` on `::`) came before `--listener` could take a host and before the log file replaced stdout as the log source; those were discarded and re-run with the final script; the first of them is what showed the `127.0.0.1` listener did not make the child fail (the reason the listener host became a parameter). The records below are from the final script, one run per row.

### What the code reads like (read, not run)

- `main.ts` `startServer` sets `PORT: String(PORT)` in the child's env, spreads `buildServerBindEnv(mode)` (HOSTNAME 127.0.0.1 when sharing is off), and in a non-packaged build spawns `pnpm dev` in `<repo root>/frontend` with `shell: true`; packaged builds use `utilityProcess.fork` of the standalone `server.js`.
- `waitForServer` (`electron/src/sharing/server-readiness.ts` lines 49 to 53) resolves on any status below 500 and sends `x-getwrite-window` on every attempt; it does not check that the responder is the forked child. `httpReadinessGet` (`main.ts` 143 to 149) is `http.get` with no timeout.
- `main.ts` `loadWhenReady` loads `localOrigin(PORT)` when readiness resolves; a child `exit` with a non-zero code calls `currentAbort`, which loads a `data:` page with the message. `requestSingleInstanceLock` is per user-data folder, so it did not interfere (a throwaway folder per run).
- `installWindowSecret` tags the window's requests to the local origin with the header.
- `getwrite:sharing-get-status` (`main.ts` ~312) builds the status from the stored setting, `process.env`, `os.networkInterfaces()` and `PORT`, behind `assertTrustedSender`; it does not consult the server.

### Results

All four runs: `isPackaged` false, the only listener on 3000 before the app launched was the throwaway listener (its pid, bind address from `lsof`), `~/Library/Logs` throwaway folder and the temp root removed at the end, 3000 free at the end.

| Run | Listener (pid, bind) | Listener requests (readiness `node-http` / window `chromium-like`) | Header present on those | Window showed | Forked child | App exited |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A. `ok`, bound `127.0.0.1` | 41976, `127.0.0.1:3000` | 1 / 1 | yes on 1 of 1 / yes on 1 of 1 | `http://localhost:3000/`, title and text `FOREIGN-LISTENER-MARKER`, from t=524, still showing it at 55 s | did **not** exit: `next dev` printed `Ready in 137ms` and listened on `*:3000` next to the listener (pid 42110 `next-server`, `lsof` showed both listeners at the end); no exit line | no |
| B. `ok`, bound `::` | 45419, `*:3000` | 1 / 1 | yes on 1 of 1 / yes on 1 of 1 | foreign page at t=496, replaced by the error page (`Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)`, plus a `Log:` path line) by t=1529 | exit code **1** at 03:52:42.921Z, 0.43 s after `server ready`; log: `Error: listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use :::3000`, `ELIFECYCLE  Command failed with exit code 1.`, `server exited with code 1` | no |
| C. `500`, bound `::` | 48900, `*:3000` | 61 / 1 | yes on 61 of 61 / **no** on 1 of 1 | error page (`Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)`) from t=816; the foreign response was never shown | exit code **1** (same EADDRINUSE lines) | no |
| D. `silent`, bound `::` | 52308, `*:3000` | 1 / 0 | yes on 1 of 1 / n/a | error page (`Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)`) from t=839 | exit code **1** (same EADDRINUSE lines) | no |

Per run, the other items asked for:

- **Message shown.** B, C and D: the window showed the `data:` page whose text is `Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)` followed by `Log: <throwaway logs folder>/getwrite.log`. A: no message; the app log has `server ready` and the dev server's own start-up lines and no error. No dialog box was opened in any run (the app uses a `data:` page for this; a native dialog was not looked for separately, and none was observed through the log or the window list, which stayed at one window).
- **App log lines (messages), A:** `app ready`, `resourcesPath`, `standaloneDir`, `projectsDir`, `templatesDir`, `Spawning dev server in: <fake root>/frontend`, `server ready` (03:51:26.429Z), the `next dev` banner, `Ready in 137ms`, `proxyClientMaxBodySize: 105906176`. No exit line in 55 s.
- **App log lines, B, C, D:** the same first six, plus `server ready` in B only (03:52:42.496Z, 0.43 s before the child's exit line), `[stderr] ⨯ Failed to start server`, `[stderr] Error: listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use :::3000` (`errno: -48`, `syscall: listen`, `address: ::`, `port: 3000`), `ELIFECYCLE  Command failed with exit code 1.`, `server exited with code 1`.
- **Sharing status** (read through the window bridge): in A and B while the window showed the foreign page it returned `enabled:false, effective:false, blockedByHostedAuth:false, port:3000, addressCount:0, credentialStore:"missing"`, so the foreign page at `http://localhost:3000/` was given the desktop bridge and the main process's trusted-sender check accepted it. At the end of B, C and D (window on a `data:` page) the call was rejected with `Refused: untrusted sender`. In A (foreign page still showing) the end read matched the earlier one. The status is built from the stored setting and `PORT` (read); in no run did it say anything about the server, so it reported nothing wrong in any run.
- **Header at the listener.** In A and B the listener received the header on the main-process readiness poll (`node-http`) and on the window's own request; in C on 61 readiness requests (the first at 364 ms, the last at 30697 ms, about every 500 ms until the 30 s deadline) and on none of its one window request; in D on its one readiness request. Presence only was recorded; no value was read.
- **Readiness kept polling after the child had exited** (C): the child's exit line was at 03:53:58.820Z, 0.4 s after launch, and the listener kept receiving the header-bearing poll for about 30 s after the error page was already up.
- **Silent listener (D):** exactly one readiness request reached the listener in 55 s; no second poll. Read: `http.get` has no timeout, so the first poll's promise never settles. The run is consistent with that reading and did not isolate it.
- **One unexplained request (C):** the listener recorded one `chromium-like` request in the 500 run with the header absent. Its time and path were not recorded (only the first four requests' details were printed), so it is not known whether it was the window's initial load, a retry, or something else. Experiment that would settle it: print time and path for every `chromium-like` request.
- **Process hygiene.** Per run the script recorded the listener pid, the Electron pid and its descendants by parent-pid recursion while alive (A 9 pids, B 8, C 5, D 5, including the listener), asked Electron to close, and then sent SIGTERM only to recorded pids still alive and with an unchanged command name. Left alive after `app.close()` in each run: the listener (all runs), and in A, besides it, two `node` and one `next-server` pids, all recorded descendants; in B the listener only; in C and D the listener only. After SIGTERM to those recorded pids, none alive in any run. `lsof` on 3000 afterwards: nothing. Pids by run (listener / Electron): A 41976 / 42013, B 45419 / 45458, C 48900 / 48939, D 52308 / 52346. (The discarded first set used other pids, stopped the same way.) `ps` afterwards showed no process whose name matches the script or temp-root prefix.

### What holds today, for Tasks 9 and 10

Measured, in dev mode on 3000 with sharing off, one run per variant:

1. **The window showed a foreign page: yes**, when the foreign listener answered 200 (A and B). It loaded in about half a second, before the forked child had finished failing. In B it stayed up for under about one second before the exit page replaced it; in A it was never replaced (see item 3).
2. **The window header reached a foreign process: yes**, on every readiness poll in all four runs (A: 1 of 1, B: 1 of 1, C: 61 of 61, D: 1 of 1), and on the window's own request in A and B. The header was recorded present or absent only; whether a foreign process could keep or reuse it was not measured, and the value was never read.
3. **A failed bind of the child is visible to the app only if the child exits non-zero.** When the child's bind failed (B, C, D: `EADDRINUSE`, exit code 1), the app showed `Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)` in under about a second. It was **not** visible when the child did not fail: in A the dev server bound `*:3000` beside a listener on `127.0.0.1:3000`, nothing failed, and the window showed the foreign page for the whole run. Cause of A not failing: **not established.** What was measured is that `next dev` printed `Network: http://10.0.0.226:3000` and `lsof` listed `*:3000` while the app had set `HOSTNAME=127.0.0.1` (read: `buildServerBindEnv`, sharing off), and that both sockets then coexisted. Experiments that would settle it: run `next dev` by hand on a free port with `HOSTNAME=127.0.0.1` and read its bind from `lsof`; run the same binary with a listener on `::` as in B; and run the packaged standalone server (`server.js` reads `HOSTNAME`) against a `127.0.0.1` listener.
4. **The readiness check does not know the child's state:** in B the app logged `server ready` and loaded the foreign page 0.43 s before the child's exit line; in C readiness kept polling, with the header, after the exit page was shown.
5. **A foreign page on the local origin gets the desktop bridge** and passes the main process's trusted-sender check (A and B, status call succeeded). Not run: whether the page's own scripts (rather than this script's `executeJavaScript`) could call the bridge methods that change sharing or devices.

The size of Task 9 (readiness accepts only the forked server) is set by items 1, 2, 4 and 5; the size of Task 10 (refuse to start when the port is busy, plain message naming the port) by item 3: today the busy port is reported to the user only as `Server exited unexpectedly (code 1)` with no port named, and only when the child's own bind fails. This record does not design either.

### Not run

- The **packaged** (unpacked) build, and the standalone `server.js` child (`utilityProcess.fork`): its bind address and its failure on a busy port were not measured. Task 19 does this on 7340.
- **Sharing on** (`sharingEnabled: true`, child bound to `0.0.0.0`, gate on): no run. Whether the child's bind then fails against each listener bind address was not measured; the gate's behaviour toward a foreign listener is not applicable (the listener is not the gate).
- Listener variants `500` and `silent` bound to `127.0.0.1`, and `ok` bound to `0.0.0.0`: not run. The `127.0.0.1` listener coexisted with the dev server in the one run that used it.
- IPv6-only resolution of `localhost` was not examined; which address `localhost` resolved to inside Chromium and inside Node's `http.get` in each run was not recorded.
- Repeats: one run per variant, so run-to-run variation (the 0.4 s timing between `server ready` and the exit line in B) is unmeasured.
- A second launch of the app against the first (`requestSingleInstanceLock`) was not run; it is per user-data folder and the runs each used their own.
- A native dialog or other user-visible message besides the `data:` page was not searched for (only the window list and the log were checked).
- Node 20 or 22; only v24.15.0.
- The discarded first set of runs is not recorded beyond the one note in Method.

## Dev commands and coexistence

**Which port: 3000.** The constant in `electron/src/server-config.ts` has not changed yet, so every Electron run here uses 3000, the port the unchanged code uses. Valid for the mechanisms; says nothing about 7340.

Task 4. Date 2026-10-10. Main checkout, branch `feat/desktop-server-port`, `git rev-parse --short HEAD` = `3e1249ee`, tree clean before (the only addition is this section and `scripts/dev-ports.mjs`). Node v24.15.0. Electron 34.5.8 (`electron/node_modules/electron`), Next.js 16.2.6 (Turbopack) as printed by `next dev`. Script: `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/dev-ports.mjs`.

### Method

- **Outside the command sandbox** (`dangerouslyDisableSandbox: true`) for every run: `ps`, `lsof`, Electron, Playwright `_electron` and `next dev` need it. No run was tried inside the sandbox, so this section does not show whether any of it would work there.
- **Pre-check:** `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` printed nothing (exit 1) before every run, and the script refuses to run otherwise. Runs were one at a time; after each, 3000 had no listener. Final check after the last run: 3000 free, no process whose command line mentions the throwaway folder name, no throwaway folder under `os.tmpdir()`, no `gw-devports*` folder under `~/Library/Logs`.
- **Listening port per command:** `lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -a -p <pid>` for every pid in the recorded set. The set is the pid the script started plus its descendants found by parent-pid recursion (`ps -A -o pid=,ppid=,comm=`) every 500 ms while alive; the listening socket is held by a descendant, as expected, so the full recorded set is listed with each result. Only recorded pids were signalled (SIGTERM; no run needed SIGKILL), never by port, and a recorded pid was signalled only if `ps` still showed the same command name for it.
- **Copied tree for every command.** A throwaway fake repo root under `os.tmpdir()`: a copy of `electron/dist` and `electron/package.json` (with a throwaway `name`, `productName` removed, so `app.getPath("logs")` is a throwaway `~/Library/Logs/gw-devports-throwaway-…` folder and not the owner's `~/Library/Logs/getwrite-electron`; the script asserted `app.getPath("logs")` equals that folder in every Electron run), a copy of the root `package.json` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, symlinks to the real `frontend/` and `getwrite-config/`, and an empty `projects/`. Every non-Electron command also had `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` set to a throwaway folder. For `pnpm dev` the working directory was the fake tree's `frontend/` (a symlink to the real `frontend/`; `.next/` is therefore the real, gitignored one); for `pnpm start` it was the fake root (and once the real repo root, see below); the Electron runs used the fake tree's `projects/` (dev mode takes `isPackaged === false` to mean `<repo root>/projects`, and that root was the fake one). This is "the development code path in a copied tree"; the difference from the repo tree itself was not measured, except that `pnpm start` was also run once from the real repo root with `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` throwaway.
- **Repo `projects/` untouched, measured:** the script prints a snapshot of the repo's `projects/` (entry count to depth 3, a SHA-1 prefix of the entry NAMES, directory mtime; no content read) before and after each run. Every run printed identical before and after: `entries(depth<=3)=703 sha1(names)=e70c2de84411 dir-mtime=2026-10-08T17:57:24.427Z`.
- **Not `pnpm electron:dev` itself.** `pnpm electron:dev` runs `tsc && pnpm run build:worker && electron dist/main.js` in `electron/`. The script ran the Electron binary directly on `<fake>/electron` (so it runs the copied `dist/main.js`) with Playwright's `_electron.launch`. `tsc` and `build:worker` were not re-run; `electron/dist` was last built by Task 3 and the commits since touch only `specs/`. That is an inference from the commit log, not a comparison of `dist` against a fresh build.
- Env for every run: `DATABASE_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`, `GETWRITE_ENABLE_DEV_IDENTITY`, `GETWRITE_BIND`, `HOSTNAME`, `GETWRITE_SHARING`, `GETWRITE_WINDOW_SECRET`, `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`, `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` (except as stated), `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` and `PORT` removed (except as stated). Sharing was left at its default (off) throughout. `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` and `~/Library/Logs/getwrite-electron` were not read, listed or written. No cookie, window-secret value, credential or pairing code was printed or stored; window text excerpts were cut to 120 characters.

### Results: the port each command LISTENS on

All four non-Electron runs used the same throwaway-projects setup and ended with 3000 free and the repo `projects/` snapshot unchanged. `HOSTNAME` was unset (the Electron run is the one that sets it, via the app).

| Command (cwd) | Started pid | Recorded set (pid ppid comm) | LISTEN row | Port | `next dev` output |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `pnpm dev` (fake tree `frontend/`) | 65330 | 65330 sh; 65350 node; 65387 node; 65393 `next-server (v16.2.6)` | pid 65393 | `*:3000` | `Local: http://localhost:3000`, `Network: http://10.0.0.226:3000`, `Ready in 133ms` |
| `pnpm start` (fake root) | 66191 | 66191 sh; 66211 node; 66248 sh; 66252 node; 66274 node; 66328 `next-server` | pid 66328 | `*:3000` | the same; first lines `getwrite@2.2.0 start …` then `pnpm --filter getwrite-frontend dev` then `next dev` |
| `pnpm start` (real repo root, `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` throwaway) | 67951 | 67951 sh; 67971 node; 68008 sh; 68012 node; 68034 node; 68040 `next-server` | pid 68040 | `*:3000` | the same |
| `pnpm dev` with `PORT=55885` (a free port the script picked with a bind to port 0; fake tree `frontend/`) | 67145 | 67145 sh; 67165 node; 67202 node; 67208 `next-server` | pid 67208 | `*:55885`; nothing on 3000 at that moment | `Local: http://localhost:55885`, `Network: http://10.0.0.226:55885` |

Measured: `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` listen on 3000 with no `PORT` set; `next dev` listens on the port in `PORT` when it is set and on nothing else (3000 had no listener while the `PORT=55885` run was up). In each case the listening socket is held by the `next-server` process, four or five levels below the pid the script started, and the wildcard address `*` was bound.

| Command | Started pid | Recorded set | LISTEN rows |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `pnpm electron:dev` equivalent (real Electron, `isPackaged` false, fake tree, no `PORT`) | 68885 (Electron main) | 68885 Electron; 68934 Electron Helper (GPU); 68935 Electron Helper; 68936 sh; 68940 node; 68959 node; 68965 `next-server (v16.2.6)`; 68982 Electron Helper (Renderer) | pid 68965: `*:3000`. pid 68885 (Electron main) also had two LISTEN rows, `127.0.0.1:55913` and `127.0.0.1:55915`. |

Measured for the Electron run: `app.isPackaged` was `false`; the window's URL was `http://localhost:3000/`, title "GetWrite", not loading; the app's own log has `Spawning dev server in: <fake>/frontend`, `Local: http://localhost:3000`, `server ready`, and then `GET / 200` and `GET /api/…` lines. The only listener on 3000 was the `next-server` in the recorded set (pid 68965). The two `127.0.0.1` ports on the Electron main process are not the app's server port; what opened them was not discriminated. A launch through Playwright is a candidate (an inspector or debugging socket), and an Electron started without Playwright and inspected with the same `lsof` command would settle it; that was not run. After `app.close()` three recorded processes were still alive (68940 node, 68959 node, 68965 `next-server`) and were stopped by those recorded pids; this matches the earlier note that dev-mode Electron leaves its server running after the window closes, here observed through `app.close()` rather than a user closing the window.

Disposition facts for the `electron:dev` path, read together with the run: `startServer` sets `PORT: String(PORT)` in the child's env (Task 3 read this); the run shows the dev server then listened on 3000, the value of `PORT`. A run with a different value of the constant has not been made (the constant has not changed); the statement that `electron:dev` follows the constant rests on the read of `main.ts` plus the `PORT` result above, not on a run with a changed constant.

### Coexistence: how the single-instance lock is keyed

- **Read:** `electron/src/main.ts` lines 764 to 778: `if (!app.requestSingleInstanceLock()) { app.quit(); } else { app.on("second-instance", …) }`, called with no `additionalData`. The comment above it says a second launch "would collide on the fixed server port". The bundled Electron 34.5.8 type documentation (`electron/node_modules/electron/electron.d.ts`, `requestSingleInstanceLock`) says the call returns `false` when another instance of the application already holds the lock, and that on macOS the system's own single-instance handling applies only to launches from Finder; it does not say what the lock is keyed by. I did not find it stated anywhere I read. The claim in the task text that Electron keys the lock by `userData` path is therefore NOT supported by what I read; the runs below are the only evidence.
- **Run, two instances with two DIFFERENT throwaway `--user-data-dir` values** (script: `node dev-ports.mjs two pw`). Both instances started from the same fake tree (same `package.json` `name`, same projects folder, same log folder), differing only in `--user-data-dir`.
  - Instance 1 (pid 70433): `hasSingleInstanceLock()` was `true`; window `http://localhost:3000/`, title "GetWrite"; the only listener on 3000 was pid 70513 (`next-server`, in instance 1's recorded set).
  - Instance 2 (launched through Playwright `_electron.launch`, pid 70567): **did not quit.** The launch succeeded and the process was still running after 40 s. Its window showed a `data:` page with the text "Server exited unexpectedly (code 1) Log: …/getw…" (cut to 120 characters). It had Electron Helper (GPU), Helper and Helper (Renderer) children (70568, 70569, 70687) and no `next-server` of its own; the only listener on 3000 after the run was still pid 70513, instance 1's.
  - The log file is shared by both instances (same app `name`), so its lines carry no instance label. Reading the 37 lines written after instance 2 started, in time order, with the attribution INFERRED from their order and not labelled in the log: a second `app ready — isPackaged: false`, `Spawning dev server in: <fake>/frontend`, `> next dev`, then `server ready` at 04:02:01.746Z, then `[stderr] ⨯ Failed to start server`, `Error: listen EADDRINUSE: address already in use :::3000` (`code: 'EADDRINUSE'`, `errno: -48`, `syscall: 'listen'`). The `server ready` line preceded the `EADDRINUSE` line by about 380 ms; Task 3 recorded the same ordering (readiness resolved on the listener that already held the port, then the forked child exited with code 1). The exit code of instance 2's forked server is "code 1" by the window text.
  - Instance 1's window afterwards was unchanged (still `http://localhost:3000/`, "GetWrite"). Neither instance's window count was more than 1.
- **Control, the SAME `--user-data-dir` for both** (script: `node dev-ports.mjs two plain same`; instance 2 started by a plain spawn so its exit status could be read): instance 2 exited at **190 ms with code 0**, signal none. Instance 1's window and server were unchanged; the only listener on 3000 remained instance 1's `next-server`. No new log line from a second app start appeared after instance 2 started (the 6 new lines were all instance 1's `GET` requests). (The script's label for the second launch in that run says "different folder"; that line is a script wording slip, the line before it says the folder was the same.)
- **Measured, stated narrowly:** in dev mode, on Electron 34.5.8 on macOS, with the same app `name` in `package.json`, the second instance quit when it shared the first one's `--user-data-dir` and did not quit when it used a different one. In the different-folder case the second app's own server failed to bind 3000 (exit code 1) and the window showed the "Server exited unexpectedly" page.
- **Hypothesis (c), stated as a hypothesis:** whether a dev app and a packaged app can run at once depends on how Electron keys its single-instance lock. What these two runs show is that, in dev mode with an unchanged `name`, the key distinguishes at least the `--user-data-dir` value. They do not show what else is in the key (for example the app name, the executable path or the bundle). Whether a packaged build and a dev build, each with its default `userData`, can run at once is NOT established. Reading only: `electron/package.json` has `"name": "getwrite-electron"` and `electron/electron-builder.yml` has `productName: GetWrite`; the owner's memory notes say the desktop `userData` is `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` (that folder was not looked at). Which default `userData` path a dev run and a packaged run each get was not measured. Experiment that would settle it: start the dev-mode app (copied tree, throwaway `--user-data-dir`) and an unpacked packaged build (throwaway `--user-data-dir`) together and record whether the second quits; and, to see whether the executable or the name matters when `--user-data-dir` is the same, start the packaged build with the dev run's `--user-data-dir`.
- **Whatever the lock does, both would want 3000 today.** Measured: with two dev-mode instances alive, the second's server could not bind 3000 (above). That is the 3000 collision for two dev-mode apps; for a dev app and a packaged app it is the same constant read by both, but it was not run.

### What this means for Task 7's question (facts only)

- `pnpm dev`, `pnpm start` and `next dev` take their port from `PORT` when it is set and otherwise listen on 3000. The Electron dev path sets `PORT` from the constant, and the dev server then listened on 3000.
- Two dev-mode app instances with different `--user-data-dir` values both start; the second's server fails to bind the shared port. With the same `--user-data-dir` the second quits.
- The packaged-plus-dev case was not run.

## Cookie scope across ports

Date 2026-10-10 (run started 22:07 MDT). Script: `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/cookie-scope.mjs` (run once, no failed or retried run). Run OUTSIDE the command sandbox (servers and Chromium). Node v24.15.0 ran the script. Browser: Playwright Chromium 148.0.7778.96 (headless, one browser, fresh contexts). Host LAN address: `<lan-ip>` (one non-internal IPv4 interface, en0; the value is not recorded here).

**Label: every request below was made from the host machine to its own LAN address. It is not a second-device result.**

### Setup (as run)

- Build: existing `frontend/.next/standalone/frontend/server.js` from Task 1; `.next/BUILD_ID` present in the standalone tree and the `static` directory present under `.next/standalone/frontend/.next/`. No rebuild.
- Before starting anything: no listener on 3000 or 7340, and none on the chosen ports. Ports chosen by binding port 0: A = 56235, B = 56236 (ephemeral ports; 3000 and 7340 were not used).
- Two instances of the same build, pids 84112 (A) and 84113 (B), no descendants found. Each with `GETWRITE_SHARING=1`, `HOSTNAME=0.0.0.0`, `GETWRITE_BIND=0.0.0.0`, the SAME throwaway `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`, a throwaway `GETWRITE_WINDOW_SECRET` (random; value never printed or recorded), own `PORT`, own throwaway `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR`. All throwaway folders were under `os.tmpdir()` (path asserted before use).
- Pairing: the script wrote `pairing-state.json` (salted HMAC of a random 6-digit code, as `frontend/scripts/sharing-refusal-smoke.mjs` does), then in a fresh browser context opened `http://<lan-ip>:56235/pair`, typed the code into the page's input and pressed Enter. The code and the cookie value were never printed.

### Measured

1. Fresh context, nothing paired, `http://<lan-ip>:A/`: 0 cookies; the navigation was redirected with status 307 to `/pair` on port A, final status 200, h1 "Pair this device", pairing input present.
2. After submitting the code at A: path became `/`. Cookie set for `<lan-ip>`: name `getwrite_device`, domain `<lan-ip>` (host-only; no port in the attribute, no leading dot), path `/`, SameSite `Strict`, HttpOnly true, Secure false, expiry present (about 365 days). Value not recorded.
3. Paired context, `http://<lan-ip>:A/`: status 200, no redirect, h1 "GetWrite", no pairing input (application page).
4. In the SAME context, before requesting B: the context's cookie jar held 1 cookie matching the URL `http://<lan-ip>:B`.
5. In the SAME context, `GET http://<lan-ip>:B/api/auth-status`: status 200, no `x-getwrite-gate` header.
6. In the SAME context, `http://<lan-ip>:B/` in the page: status 200, no redirect, final path `/` on port B, h1 "GetWrite", no pairing input, body text length 603 (the same as at A); that is the application page, not the pairing page.
7. Cookie attributes after requesting B: still one cookie `getwrite_device`, domain `<lan-ip>`, path `/`, SameSite Strict, HttpOnly true, Secure false, expiry present (about 365 days). No second cookie appeared.
8. Control, a second fresh context never paired, `http://<lan-ip>:B/`: 307 to `/pair` on port B, final 200, h1 "Pair this device", pairing input present.

### Separate items

- **`localStorage` and `sessionStorage` (from the run):** after setting a marker key in both at origin A, A held 1 key in each; at B (same context, same host, other port) each held 0 keys and the marker was absent; back at A each still held 1 key with the marker present. Measured: both storages were separate per port in this run. (The page at B was also an application page, and it added no key of its own at the time of reading.)
- **Saved bookmark (reasoning, not run):** a bookmark or saved home-screen link at `http://<lan-ip>:<old port>` names a different origin from `http://<lan-ip>:<new port>`; opening it requests the old port, where this feature leaves no listener, so it does not reach the server on the new port. What a given browser or phone shows for that failed request was not exercised here (Task 6 covers Chromium for 3000). The bookmark itself was not created or opened in this run.

### Verdict (this run only)

In this run, the device paired at `http://<lan-ip>:56235` was treated as paired at `http://<lan-ip>:56236`: the same context got status 200 on `/api/auth-status` and the application page (not the pairing page) at B, with a single host-only cookie that has no port in its scope, against the same credential store. A never-paired context at B was sent to the pairing page, so the result is not a property of B refusing nothing.

FR-14 hypothesis (b), "browsers scope cookies by host and not by port, so a device paired at one port may still be paired at another on the same host": **confirmed by this run**, for Chromium 148 headless, plain HTTP, one host address, two instances of one build sharing one `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`. The cookie being sent to B and accepted by the shared store is observed; the run did not separate which of the two (cookie scoping in the browser, the shared store) would alone be enough, because neither was varied. Not named as a cause beyond what was observed.

### Not run

- Any other browser (Firefox, WebKit, desktop Chrome, Safari, Android Chrome) and a real second device (this is Task 21 for the phone).
- The packaged or dev Electron app and the numbers 7340 and 3000 (ports were ephemeral; the Host allowlist matched `<lan-ip>:<port>` for each instance's own `PORT`).
- HTTPS, a `.local` hostname, a different LAN address for the same host, IPv6.
- A browser holding a cookie from a store that does not contain its hash (the shared store was the same for A and B; a different store at B was not run).
- The bookmark case itself; the cookie surviving a browser restart; cookie renewal (`Max-Age`) at B as a separate check (expiry present, about 365 days, was read, not compared before and after).
- Node 20.

### Cleanup

Pids started: 84112, 84113 (no descendants found); both stopped by pid; none left alive. The throwaway folder under `os.tmpdir()` was removed. After the run no listener on A, B, 3000 or 7340. `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` and `~/Library/Logs/getwrite-electron` were not read, listed or written. No cookie, secret or code value was printed or recorded.

## Old address and browser acceptance of 7340

Task 6. Date 2026-10-10. Main checkout, branch `feat/desktop-server-port`. Script: `specs/features/desktop-server-port/scripts/old-port-and-7340.mjs` (run once; no failed or retried run). Run OUTSIDE the command sandbox (`next dev`, Chromium and listeners). Node v24.15.0 ran the script. Browser: Playwright Chromium 148.0.7778.96, headless, a fresh context per visit. Next.js 16.2.6 (Turbopack) as printed by `next dev`. The constant in `electron/src/server-config.ts` has not changed; 3000 is still the app's port. `<lan-ip>` is the host's one non-internal IPv4 address (the value is withheld and shown this way throughout).

### Pre-check

Before the script started anything, `lsof -nP -iTCP:3000 -sTCP:LISTEN` and `lsof -nP -iTCP:7340 -sTCP:LISTEN` each printed nothing (run by hand, then again by the script, which refuses to run otherwise). Nothing the task did not start was listening on either port. This is the only task in the list that deliberately places a throwaway listener on 7340.

### Measured

**1. Nothing on 3000 (checked in the script: no listener), Chromium 148.0.7778.96:**

- `http://<lan-ip>:3000/`: navigation failed with `page.goto: net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED at http://<lan-ip>:3000/`. The page URL afterwards was `about:blank` and its body text was unreadable.
- `http://localhost:3000/`: the same, `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED at http://localhost:3000/`.
- The text of Chromium's own error page was NOT captured: in this headless run the failed navigation left the page at `about:blank`, so what a headed Chromium or a phone browser prints for the same failure was not read here. What is recorded is the error code Chromium reported to Playwright.

**2. Throwaway `next dev` on 3000, sharing off.** Command: `pnpm dev` with cwd the frontend of a copied fake repo root (a copy of the root `package.json` and `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, symlinks to the real `frontend/` and `getwrite-config/`), with `GETWRITE_SHARING`, `HOSTNAME`, `PORT`, `GETWRITE_BIND`, `GETWRITE_WINDOW_SECRET`, `GETWRITE_SHARING_DIR`, `DATABASE_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` unset (sharing off), `GETWRITE_PROJECTS_DIR` a throwaway empty folder under `os.tmpdir()`. This is a `next dev` of the repo's frontend, not the Electron app.

- Pids recorded: `pnpm` shell chain 89341 (sh), 89361 (node), 89398 (node), 89404 (`next-server (v16.2.6)`). The listener on 3000 was pid 89404, bound `*:3000`, within the recorded set (checked before any request). `next dev` printed `Local: http://localhost:3000` and `Network: http://<lan-ip>:3000`.
- **Label: both requests below were made from the host machine to its own LAN address (Node `http.get`). They are NOT a second-device result.** Whether this behaviour predates Feature 75 was not checked.
- `GET http://<lan-ip>:3000/api/projects`: status 200, `x-getwrite-gate` response header absent, content-type `application/json`, body a JSON array of length 0 (the throwaway projects folder was empty). Shape only; no content recorded.
- `GET http://<lan-ip>:3000/`: status 200, `x-getwrite-gate` absent, content-type `text/html; charset=utf-8`, body length 36646.
- So the dev server answered a request addressed to the host's LAN address with its normal response, not a refusal. The empty array means the response carried no project data because there was none to carry; a request returning data was not tried.
- Stopped by pid: SIGTERM to the four recorded pids; none alive afterwards; no listener on 3000.

**3. Throwaway plain HTTP server on 7340.** This file started as its own process (`--listener`), `0.0.0.0:7340`, status 200 with a one-line marker page (`PORT-7340-MARKER-PAGE`). Pid 89822; `lsof` showed it as the only listener on 7340 (`*:7340`).

- Chromium 148.0.7778.96, `http://<lan-ip>:7340/`: loaded, status 200, title and body text the marker. **Accepted: yes.**
- Chromium 148.0.7778.96, `http://localhost:7340/`: loaded, status 200, same marker. **Accepted: yes.**
- Stopped by pid (SIGTERM); none alive; no listener on 7340 afterwards.

### What this does and does not show

- Chromium 148 (Playwright build, headless) loaded plain HTTP pages on 7340 from both the LAN address and localhost, from a server that is not the GetWrite server. The page was a marker page, not the pairing page or an application page; the GetWrite pages on 7340 belong to Tasks 17 to 19.
- The old-address failure for Chromium with nothing on 3000 was a refused connection, reported as `net::ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`. Whether that failure is "confusing" for a writer (OQ-5) is a judgment for the Task 7 stop; this record does not decide it.
- A development server on 3000 answered the host's own LAN address with 200 and no gate header in this run. The Task 4 finding that dev-mode `next dev` listens on `*:3000` is consistent with it; no cause for the answer beyond that is claimed.

### Not checked (browsers)

Firefox, desktop Chrome and Safari: **not checked**. The statement in the spec's scope note that 7340 is absent from Chrome's and Firefox's restricted-port lists remains unchecked memory for them. The Android phone result is not claimed here (Task 21).

### Not run

- Any browser other than Playwright's headless Chromium 148; a headed Chromium (and so the text of its error page); the Android phone or any second device.
- The real Electron app and the GetWrite server on 7340; the pairing page and an application page on 7340.
- A request to the dev server on 3000 that returns real project data; a dev server with sharing on; the repo tree's own `pnpm dev` (the copied tree was used).
- Whether the dev server's LAN answer predates Feature 75.
- IPv6, a `.local` hostname, HTTPS, a different LAN address.

Pids started and stopped by pid: 89341, 89361, 89398, 89404 (next dev tree), 89822 (7340 listener); none left alive. After the run: no listener on 3000 or 7340 (`lsof` printed nothing for each, also checked by the script). The throwaway folder under `os.tmpdir()` was removed. The repo's `projects/` name-hash was `796ca22ebbca` (17 names) before and after. `~/Library/Application Support/getwrite-electron/` and `~/Library/Logs/getwrite-electron` were not read, listed or written. ## Owner decisions at the Task 7 stop

Date 2026-10-10. Recorded by the pipeline lead. The lead put the measurements of Tasks 1 to 6 to the owner with a recommendation on each point; the owner's answer was "your recs". The decisions:

1. **Dev mode (OQ-2).** The dev desktop app follows the constant to 7340 (it already passes `PORT` to its dev server). Plain `pnpm dev` and `pnpm start` stay on 3000. No separate dev port: Task 13 is closed as not needed. Known cost, accepted: a dev desktop app and a packaged app both want 7340; whether the two can otherwise run together was not measured.
2. **Already-paired devices (OQ-4).** `device-credentials.json` is left alone; no migration code. One documentation line: a paired device normally stays paired on the new port (measured in Chromium only, Task 5), and if it does not, it is paired again. The owner's phone check covers Android Chrome.
3. **Old address (OQ-5).** One documentation line only. No listener or redirector on 3000. Task 14 (a sharing-panel sentence) is closed as not needed.
4. **Scope of Tasks 9 and 10, confirmed from the Task 3 measurement.** (a) Before forking the server the app checks that nothing is listening on the port on any address, and if something is, refuses to start with the message naming the port (this covers the measured case where a listener on `127.0.0.1` and the app's server on the wildcard address coexisted and nothing failed). (b) Readiness accepts only the server the app forked, by a per-launch value the server echoes; the window secret is sent to nothing until that is confirmed. (c) The window loads the local origin only after that confirmation, so a foreign page is never loaded into a window that has the desktop bridge. (d) Readiness polling stops when the forked server exits, and each poll has a timeout. Task 9's estimate goes from 5 to 8; Task 10 stays at 5.

Correction by the lead: before Task 5 the lead told the owner that a paired device would have to pair again after a port change because the cookie "belongs to the host and port". Task 5 measured the opposite in Chromium (one host-only cookie, accepted on a second port against the same store).
